The Project Σ guide

Everything Project Σ does, one area per page, with every option it offers. Inside the app, More Options (the ⋮ button at the top right) > User Guide opens it in a new tab.

Beyond the user manual

How this guide is written

  • Names in bold are exactly what the app shows: a button, a menu item, a tab, a setting. A path such as Settings > General > Library Backup means: open Settings, pick General, find the Library Backup group.
  • More Options is the ⋮ menu at the right end of the top bar. On a phone it is More in the bottom bar.
  • Keys look like Esc. Things you type, such as search prefixes and macros, look like creator:.

Project Σ

A self-hosted home for your AI character cards. Project Σ keeps your characters, their chats, lorebooks, personas and gallery media in one library on your own machine, lets you search it like a database, chat with your characters through your own AI connection, and browse the popular character sites without leaving the app.

Project Σ is the successor to the Character Library extension for SillyTavern: you can see it as a standalone Character Library.

It runs as a small server on your computer or a home server, and you use it from any browser on your network, phones included. Everything lives in one data/ folder. The library is built to stay quick at sizes that would stall a folder of card files: it is designed for libraries of a million characters.

This page is the tour and the common questions; the user guide covers every feature and option. The guide is one page that opens in any browser: online at that link, in the app under More Options > User Guide, or docs/guide.html in the install folder.

What it does

  • A library that scales. A fast grid with eleven sort orders, favorites, playlists with icons and colours, and a Tags Manager that renames, merges and cleans up tags across the whole library. Select one character or every character in the current view and tag, favorite, export or delete them in one go. Find duplicates, and ask an AI connection to recommend characters from your own library.
  • Search that understands cards. Press the key under Esc anywhere for Search everything, one box over your characters, chats, messages, lorebook entries and settings. In the library, search names, tags, creators, notes or card text; narrow with prefixes such as creator:, fav:yes or chub:no; and build Advanced Filters from more than twenty fields (token count, last chat, linked lorebook, alternate greetings and more), saved as presets.
  • Every card detail, editable. Read and edit every field of a card, its embedded and linked lorebooks, and its picture. Each edit can be snapshotted first, so version history shows a field-by-field diff and restores any earlier state.
  • Chats that run here. A full conversation screen: streaming replies, alternates you swipe through, continue, regenerate, "write my next message", branching a chat from any message, hidden messages, thinking shown in a fold, and export to JSONL. Attach pictures to a message when the model sees them, and keep a gallery for each chat beside its character's. Around the conversation sit a prompt manager (it imports SillyTavern chat-completion presets and builds them the way SillyTavern does), regex scripts, a header you arrange yourself, a prompt inspector that shows exactly what is sent, an Author's note, chat backgrounds, and an out-of-character assistant that knows your persona and the Author's note and can propose lorebook, message, card, note and persona edits for you to review. See also chat windows.
  • Your own AI connection. Any endpoint that speaks the OpenAI-compatible chat API, local or hosted, plus Anthropic's API through a bundled extension. Keys stay on the server and are never sent back to the browser.
  • Lorebooks. A manager with every entry option, books linked to characters, bound to one chat, tied to a persona or active everywhere, a chat window that shows which entries fired and why, and AI generation of entries from pasted text, a wiki page or your own characters.
  • Personas with pronouns. Be someone different everywhere or in one chat only, with a picture, bound lorebooks and pronouns that cards can use through {{sub}}, {{obj}} and friends.
  • A creator with a design partner. Write a card from scratch or start from one in your library, with a Studio that talks the whole draft through with you and proposes changes you apply one by one.
  • The character sites in one tab. Browse ChubAI, JanitorAI, JannyAI, CharacterTavern, Pygmalion, Wyvern, Botbooru, DataCat and the RisuAI Realm; see at a glance what you already own; import in the background; keep linked characters up to date with a field-by-field update check.
  • Media that stays put. Each character has a gallery for images, video and audio, a full-screen viewer, and a downloader that pulls the remote images a card links to, including whole albums from common image hosts, so nothing breaks when a host goes away.
  • Make it yours. A theme editor with presets and a live preview, a custom CSS editor, and a CSS assistant.
  • Extensions. A bundled set (memory summaries, trackers, a translator, story beats, ratings and notes, library stats, and more), each off until you switch it on, and installable extensions from a git URL or a zip. The bundled set is vibe-coded: written entirely by AI as a stress test of the extension API, not reviewed line by line, and meant as examples rather than core features. An installed extension runs with full access to your server, every account on it and the computer it runs on, and Project Σ does not review extensions or answer for what they do: read Before you install anything first.
  • Built to be trusted with your library. Optional accounts with a separate library each, scheduled database backups, a consistency checker, and a migration that brings a whole SillyTavern library across and verifies it. See also accounts.
  • An API underneath. Everything the app does goes through a documented HTTP API: power users can automate tasks and script their own library with it (bulk tagging and cleanup, scheduled imports and exports, backups on a timer, reports on the collection), and extension creators build on it.

Quick start

Requires Node.js 22.15 or newer. Get the code with git:

git clone https://github.com/Sillyanonymous/ProjectSigma.git
cd ProjectSigma

or with Code > Download ZIP on GitHub, unpacked. Then, in that folder:

npm install
npm start

Or let a launcher do it: double-click Start Project Sigma.bat on Windows, or run sh start.sh on Linux, macOS and Android (in Termux; the guide has the steps). It checks that Node.js is new enough, installs what Project Σ needs on the first run and again after an update, starts the server and opens your browser on it (openBrowser: false in data/server.yaml keeps it from that). Keep its window open while you use the app; closing it stops the server.

Open http://localhost:7788 and the app loads. All state lives in the data/ folder next to the install. To use it from your phone, open the same address with this computer's network address in place of localhost.

With Docker instead: docker compose -f docker/compose.yaml up -d from the install folder. docker/README.md covers the volumes, updating, and the optional browser for the sites behind Cloudflare.

New to it? Getting started walks through the first ten minutes.

Bringing a SillyTavern library

Project Σ can bring a whole SillyTavern user folder across (the folder holding characters/, chats/ and worlds/, usually data/default-user): cards with their tags and favorites, chats, lorebooks and their links, gallery media, version snapshots, playlists, personas, custom CSS, saved prompts and filter presets. The SillyTavern folder is only ever read, never changed.

The easy way is the wizard: Settings > Account > Bring your SillyTavern library. Point it at the folder, press Check to read the plan, run it, and finalize when you are satisfied. Re-running is always safe until you finalize: every character heals in place and nothing duplicates.

From a terminal, the same tool:

npm run migrate -- <path-to-st-user-dir> --user <handle> --dry-run
npm run migrate -- <path-to-st-user-dir> --user <handle>
npm run migrate -- <path-to-st-user-dir> --user <handle> --finalize

The first prints the plan and writes nothing; the second runs the migration and finishes by verifying it against the source, field by field and message by message; the third verifies one last time and makes the migrated characters indistinguishable from ones created here. Group chats, SillyTavern's own settings and its asset folders stay behind. Every flag, the exit codes and the full list of what comes across are in Bringing a SillyTavern library.

FAQ

Is this a SillyTavern extension?

No. It is a standalone app with its own server and database. It can import a SillyTavern data folder, and chats export in SillyTavern's JSONL format, but it does not need SillyTavern installed.

Do I need an AI model to use it?

No. The library, search, editing, lorebooks, online browsing and media all work without one. Chatting, the assistant, the Studio, the Card Recommender, AI lorebook generation, the CSS assistant and some extensions need a connection, which you add under Settings > AI.

Which AI services work?

Any endpoint that offers the OpenAI-compatible chat completions API, which covers most hosted services and local servers; the endpoint you enter includes the API's version path, for example ending in /v1. Anthropic's own API works through the bundled Anthropic extension.

Is my API key safe?

It is stored on the server, in your library's database, and the browser never receives it back: the app only ever shows "Key stored" and its last four characters. Treat your data/ folder and its backups as you would any file holding a key.

Can I use it on my phone?

Yes. The app switches to a phone layout with a bottom bar, swipe gestures and full-screen sheets. Open the server's address from the phone's browser while both are on the same network.

Can several people share one install?

Yes. Turn on accounts (multiUser: true in data/server.yaml) and each person gets a separate library, settings and media. Without accounts there is no sign-in, so anyone who can reach the port can use and change everything. Accounts and server settings

Which card formats does it read?

Card PNGs (V1, V2 and V3) and CHARX files, plus Project Σ's own bundles. It exports card PNGs and full bundles with chats and gallery media. JSON card files are not imported.

Does it do group chats?

No. Every chat has one character; a SillyTavern migration counts group chats and leaves them where they are.

How do I back up?

Settings > General > Library Backup takes a copy of the database on demand or on a daily or weekly schedule. It does not cover images, so copy the whole data/ folder now and then too. Data and backups

Something broke after I turned on an extension

Switch it off under Settings > Extensions. If the app will not open far enough for that, start the server with CL_EXTENSIONS=off: the app runs with every extension dark while you remove the broken one's folder from extensions/installed/. Troubleshooting covers this and the other common snags.

Should I use a theme, Custom CSS or a theme extension?

For colours, corners, spacing and fonts, use the theme editor: it is quick, follows you to every device and keeps working across updates. For anything else (one component, a picture, hiding something), use Custom CSS snippets, which take any CSS but can stop matching when an update changes the app's markup. A theme extension packages a look for every account on a server, and a full one like the bundled Midnight or Σ adds its own settings, palettes and motion. Themes, Custom CSS and theme extensions compares them in full.

Configuration

Server settings live in data/server.yaml, which the first boot writes with a comment on every setting. Where a setting has an environment variable, the variable wins over the file.

server.yaml keyEnvironment variableDefaultWhat it sets
multiUserCL_MULTI_USER (true or false)falseSign-in and a separate home per account
sessionTimeoutDays30How long a sign-in lasts, in days; -1 for no limit (a browser still keeps the sign-in at most 400 days)
portCL_PORT7788The port the server listens on
hostCL_HOST0.0.0.0The address it binds: every interface by default, so a phone on your network can reach it; 127.0.0.1 keeps it to this machine
corsCL_CORS (comma-separated)noneThe other origins whose pages may call the API, such as a dashboard or tool of your own served from another address (* for any)
hostsCL_HOSTS (comma-separated)noneWith multi-user off, the machine names the server answers besides IP addresses and localhost, so a page on another site that points its own name here is refused
openBrowserCL_OPEN_BROWSER (true or false)trueWhether the launchers open the browser once the server answers; false for a server run headless, as a service, or with a tab already open

These are set only in the environment:

Environment variableDefaultWhat it sets
CL_DATA_DIRdata/ in the installWhere every library, account and server.yaml itself live, which is why the file cannot move it
CL_EXTENSIONS_DIRextensions/installed/ in the installWhere installed extensions and their .data/ scratch folders live
CL_EXTENSIONSonoff boots with every extension dark: the way back in when one breaks the app
CL_BROWSERa Playwright Chromium, else Chrome or Edge, found on the machineThe browser the hosted-browser services launch, tried before any found

The server reads server.yaml strictly: a setting it does not know, or a value of the wrong kind (multiUser: yes, a sessionTimeoutDays that is not a number), stops the boot with a message naming the setting, rather than starting a server configured differently from what was meant. The yes-or-no and number variables are read the same way (CL_MULTI_USER=1 stops the boot too).

For power users and extension creators

  • The API: everything the app does goes through the public HTTP API, so you can automate tasks and script your own library: bulk tagging and cleanup, scheduled imports and exports, backups on a timer, reports on the collection, or your library feeding tools of your own. The live spec is at GET /api/openapi.json; a browsable reference is docs/api-docs.html (regenerate it with npm run docs). The API covers signing in, calls from another site and the live change feed.
  • Extensions: the extension API gives extensions the same reach as the app's own code: events, storage, settings, windows, views and providers. The extension authors' guide, the user guide's last part, is the reference.
  • Checking a library: npm run verify reads every library in data/ against the consistency rules the server keeps (foreign keys, the search indexes, the maintained counts, the files behind their rows) and exits non-zero naming any finding. npm run verify -- --user <handle> checks one account; --db-only skips the walk over the files on disk, the one check whose time grows with a large media folder. --repair <check> fixes what a check found, for the checks that have a repair, after taking a backup; run it with the server stopped. It also lists who owns each table of a library (the app, each extension, or nobody), and --drop-leftover <id> drops the tables a removed extension left behind, after asking and taking a backup.
  • No build step: the client is plain JS, CSS and HTML served as is.

Issues are welcome; pull requests are closed. CONTRIBUTING.md says how to report a problem and why an extension is the way to change the app.

License

Source available for personal use; see LICENSE for the terms and NOTICE for the bundled third-party components. The license is deliberately narrow for now, to protect the time and work behind the project while it finds its feet; the intent is to loosen it in the future. Forking the repository on GitHub to run and change for yourself is fine; a fork keeps this license, so it stays non-commercial and is not re-hosted or published elsewhere. The extension API is the open door: extensions are your own work, under whatever license you choose, and the bundled extensions are MIT, so any of them can be the start of yours.

Getting started

Installing and starting

Project Σ needs Node.js 22.15 or newer. Get the code with git:

git clone https://github.com/Sillyanonymous/ProjectSigma.git
cd ProjectSigma

or download it from GitHub with Code > Download ZIP and unpack it. Then, in that folder:

npm install
npm start

A launcher does the same for you: double-click Start Project Sigma.bat on Windows, or run sh start.sh on Linux and macOS. It checks that Node.js is new enough, installs what Project Σ needs on the first run and again after an update, starts the server and opens your browser on it. Keep its window open while you use the app; closing it stops the server. To start without opening the browser (a server you run headless, or with a tab already open), set openBrowser: false in data/server.yaml.

The server prints the address it listens on (port 7788 unless you change it). Open http://localhost:7788 in a browser and the app loads. Everything you add is kept in the data/ folder next to the install; nothing is sent anywhere unless you browse an online site, chat through a connection you set up, or install an extension or look for its updates.

To stop it, stop the npm start process. To change the port, the address it listens on, or turn on accounts, see the configuration table in the README.

To run it with Docker instead, docker compose -f docker/compose.yaml up -d in the same folder starts it in a container, with everything you add kept in a Docker volume. The docker folder's own README covers updating, the volumes, and the optional browser for the sites behind Cloudflare.

Running it on an Android phone

Project Σ runs on an Android phone inside Termux. Install Termux from F-Droid or GitHub (the Play Store version is not maintained), then in Termux:

pkg update && pkg upgrade
pkg install git nodejs-lts python make clang
git clone https://github.com/Sillyanonymous/ProjectSigma.git
cd ProjectSigma
sh start.sh

Use the launcher rather than npm install: phones have no native build of the image library, and the launcher installs the portable one for you. The first start builds the database driver on the phone, which takes a few minutes. If that build stops with "Undefined variable android_ndk_path", run mkdir -p ~/.gyp && echo "{'variables':{'android_ndk_path':''}}" > ~/.gyp/include.gypi and then sh start.sh again.

Once it is running, open http://localhost:7788 in the phone's browser. Android pauses apps in the background, so run termux-wake-lock first if the server should keep running with the screen off.

Opening it on your phone

The server listens on every network interface by default, so any device on your network can open it: use this computer's network address instead of localhost, for example http://192.168.1.20:7788. Bookmark it, or add it to the home screen, and it behaves like an app.

Without accounts there is no sign-in, so anyone who can reach that address can use the library. If other people share your network, turn accounts on or keep the server to this machine; Accounts and server settings explains both.

The first screen

A new library is empty, and the grid offers three ways in:

  • Import Characters opens Import Characters on its Local Files tab, where you drop card files; its URLs tab takes links. See Importing and exporting.
  • Bring a SillyTavern Library opens the migration wizard. See Bringing a SillyTavern library.
  • Browse Online opens the Online view, where you browse the character sites and import with one click. See Browsing online.

Finding your way around

The top bar holds everything:

  1. The view switcher: Characters (your library), Chats (every conversation) and Online (the character sites). Extensions can add views of their own, which appear after these, and a narrow window folds the extra ones behind a ⋯ button.
  2. The search box. See Search and filters.
  3. Advanced Filters.
  4. Search Settings: which fields the search reads.
  5. The sort.
  6. Tags: filters by tags.
  7. Playlists: filters by a playlist.
  8. More Options, the menu for everything else, below.

On Chats the view's own filters are the sort, the grouping and the layout (see The Chats view). Whatever does not fit folds behind More filters. A bell appears beside More Options while something is running or needs you; see Notifications.

ItemWhat it opens
Search EverythingOne field for your characters, chats, lorebooks, settings and actions (Search everything)
Import CharactersCard files, links and bundles (Importing and exporting)
LorebooksThe Lorebooks manager (Lorebooks)
Tags ManagerRename, merge and clean up tags (The library)
Card RecommenderAI suggestions from your own library (The library)
Create CharacterThe character creator (Creating characters)
Find DuplicatesCharacters that look like the same card (The library)
Auto-Link CharactersMatch unlinked characters to their online source (Browsing online)
RefreshReload the grid, keeping your filters
User GuideThis guide, in a new tab
SettingsEverything configurable (Settings and themes)

Import Characters, Find Duplicates, Auto-Link Characters and Refresh belong to the Characters view and hide in the others. Extensions can add their own items at the bottom, under Extensions.

The start page and view defaults

Settings > General > Start Page > Open on picks the view the app opens on: Characters, Chats, Online, or a view an extension adds. It follows your account, so every device you sign in from opens there.

Settings > General > View Defaults has a tab per view with what that view starts with:

ViewSettingChoicesDefault
CharactersSortthe eleven sort ordersName (A-Z)
CharactersGroup favorites firston or offoff
CharactersFilter preset(None) or a saved Advanced Filter preset(None)
ChatsSortthe seven chat sort ordersMost Recent
ChatsViewRecent, by time or By characterRecent, by time
OnlineProviderFirst Available or any siteFirst Available

Changes in the top bar (a different sort, a search) last for the session; saving Settings puts every view back on these defaults. The Online tab opens on its default provider only the first time; after that it stays on the one you last browsed.

On a phone

On a small screen or a touch-first device the app switches to its phone layout:

  • The top bar gives way to a bottom bar with Characters, Chats, Online, a sliders button for filters and sort, and More for the More Options menu, notifications and any extension views.
  • A round search button floats above the bottom bar on the three main views.
  • Dialogs open as full-screen sheets; drag one down, or use the phone's back button, to close it.
  • Swipe left or right on the main screen to change views; on a character card, swipe right to favorite it and left for its menu.

Settings > General > Theme & Layout > Mobile Layout forces it: Auto (Default) decides by screen and device, Always and Never override. The choice is kept per device. Every gesture is listed in Keyboard shortcuts and gestures.

Getting help

More Options > User Guide opens this guide in a new tab; the search at its top finds any section. Tips and tricks gathers the features that are easy to miss, and Troubleshooting covers the common problems.

Troubleshooting

The server and the page

The server will not start and names a setting

The server reads data/server.yaml strictly. A setting it does not know, or a value of the wrong kind (multiUser: yes instead of true), stops it with a message naming the setting. Fix that line, or the environment variable it names, and start again. The settings are listed in the README.

A page says the server "does not answer" to a name

Without accounts, the server answers only requests addressed to an IP address or localhost. If you reach it by a machine name, add the name to hosts in server.yaml (or CL_HOSTS) and restart. See Accounts and server settings.

My phone cannot reach it

Open the computer's network address with the port (for example http://192.168.1.20:7788), with both devices on the same network. Check that host in server.yaml is not 127.0.0.1, and that the computer's firewall lets the port through.

A library "was written by a newer version of the server"

The library was opened by a newer version of Project Σ, which upgraded it. Update this install to that version or newer. See Upgrading.

I forgot my password

An admin resets it with Issue a temporary password under Settings > Account > Users. See Managing accounts.

Open tabs after an update

A tab keeps the app it loaded. After a server restart it reconnects by itself and reads everything again, but after an update, reload every open tab (a phone's included) to get the new version.

A save did not go through

When saving a character fails, the message says why and your edits stay in the details window, so you can save again. A setting that could not be saved goes back to what the server holds, and a message says so.

The library

The first browse is slow

Thumbnails are made the first time each picture is shown. Settings > Media > Bulk Operations > Generate Missing Thumbnails makes them all ahead of time.

The disk is busy and searches are a little slow

Once a big import, a SillyTavern migration, an update or everyday edits have left it in pieces, Project Σ tidies its search index while the app sits idle, and pauses when you click, type or scroll again. A tidy-up you start yourself with Tidy Now keeps going while you work, from seconds to several minutes on a large library on a hard drive. The bell shows Maintenance while one runs, and everything is back to full speed once it is done. See Keeping it that fast and Search Index.

A search finds nothing I expected

Check Search Settings (the sliders by the search box): plain text only searches the ticked fields, and with none ticked nothing matches. Look for an active tag filter, playlist, Advanced Filter rule or Favorites only too. See Search and filters.

An import says the character is already in my library

Imports never overwrite. An identical card is skipped; a card that differs from yours offers Import as Copy. See Importing and exporting.

A card's images are broken

The images are on a host that removed them or blocks them. If you downloaded them earlier, switch on Replace remote URLs with local files; otherwise press Download Media on the Gallery tab while they still exist. See Media and galleries.

"Changed elsewhere" appeared

Another tab or device saved the same character or chat. Press Reload to see the newer version; your unsaved edits are kept until you confirm.

Chatting

"No connection is configured yet." or "cannot send yet"

Add an AI connection, or finish the one the note names: Add a Connection and Finish Setting It Up open Connections in the chat, and Settings > AI has the same list (see Connections). A connection needs an endpoint (unless its kind brings its own) and a model.

Replies fail or come back empty

The reason shows above the message box:

  • The model spent its whole reply budget reasoning: lower Reasoning effort or raise Max reply tokens on the connection.
  • An empty reply or a timeout: the endpoint did not answer in time. Test in the connection editor checks it.
  • The prompt does not fit: the card, lore and examples alone exceed the context budget. Raise the connection's Context size (or Context budget in Chat settings), or trim the lore budget.

The prompt inspector shows exactly what is being sent.

The character forgets early messages

Messages older than the Context starts here divider do not fit the budget and are not sent. Raise the budget if your model allows it, or use the bundled Memory extension, which keeps a running summary in the prompt.

{{user}} shows up literally

You have no persona yet. Create one under Settings > Chat > Personas. See Personas.

Online sites

A site will not load

Press Retry. If it keeps failing, the site may have changed or be down; Copy Error Report gives a summary to paste into a bug report (your settings appear only as on/off, never your tokens).

JanitorAI or JannyAI say they need a browser

Both sit behind Cloudflare. Set up Settings > Online > Hosted Browser, then press Check janitorai.com or Check jannyai.com. If this machine cannot get through, point the Hosted Browser at a browser on another machine. See The Hosted Browser.

JanitorAI only shows one page

Sign in under Settings > Online > JanitorAI > Step 2: Account Login.

A JannyAI import came in almost empty

Without the Hosted Browser, JannyAI imports carry only the name, picture, tags and blurb. Set the browser up, then import again.

A link names a site that is not in the list

A character linked to a site the app does not offer, or to one whose extension is off or removed, keeps its link. The link chip in its details shows the site's name with a plug icon, and the update check passes it by. Click the chip to remove the link, or leave it: it reconnects by itself if the site returns, for example through an extension.

Extensions and looks

An extension broke something

Switch it off in Settings > Extensions. If the app will not open far enough, start the server with CL_EXTENSIONS=off. See Extensions.

My custom CSS made the app unusable

Open the browser's developer console and run clResetCSS(). It clears the active custom CSS and keeps your snippets.

Windows open in odd places or sizes

Settings > General > Theme & Layout > Reset Window Layouts.

Keyboard shortcuts and gestures

Keyboard shortcuts

Single-key shortcuts never fire while you are typing in a box.

Changing a key

The keys of the app's commands can be changed under Settings > Keyboard: Search everything, the library's selection mode, a conversation's /, End, R, C, I, E and Shift+Esc, the chat Gallery's keys and the gallery viewer's 0. Click a key, then press the new one: Esc cancels, and Backspace leaves the shortcut without a key. A key another shortcut already uses in the same place asks whether to swap them. Each row has its own reset, and Reset Every Key puts them all back. Your keys follow your account to every device; Ctrl and Cmd count as the same key, so a key set on Windows works on a Mac. An extension with shortcuts of its own lists them there under its name.

Esc, Enter, Tab, Backspace and the arrow keys keep their jobs everywhere: none of them can be a shortcut's key on its own, though held with Ctrl, Alt or Shift they can. On a phone, Keyboard appears in Settings once a keyboard is connected and you press a key. The tables below show the keys as they start.

Everywhere

KeyDoes
EscCloses the top window, dialog or menu (asking first if it holds unsaved edits)
The key under EscOpens or closes Search everything

The key under Esc, left of the 1, is found by its place, whatever your keyboard prints on it: ` on a US keyboard, ^ on a German one, ² on a French one, § on a Nordic one.

Search everything

KeyDoes
↑ / ↓Moves through the results
EnterOpens the highlighted result, or runs the highlighted action
TabShows what else you can do with the highlighted result
EscSteps back out of an action, then closes
BackspaceIn an empty field, steps back out of an action

The library

KeyDoes
SpaceStarts or ends selecting characters (see Selecting many characters)

Home / EndJumps to the top or bottom of the grid
EnterIn a tag box, adds the tag; in a playlist or preset name box, creates or saves it

Character details

↓ / ↑In Add tag..., lights a suggestion; Enter adds the one lit (the same in Create Character and the Card Recommender's tag boxes)

The gallery viewer

A conversation

← / →Pages the last reply's alternates; past the last one, writes another

In the chat's Gallery window: ← / → step, G grid, / search, F full screen, B set as background, A attach to your next message, N creator's notes, + / - zoom, 0 reset.

In the prompt inspector's search, Enter finds the next match and Shift+Enter the previous one. In the swipe combiner, Ctrl+Enter generates.

On a computer, a window's resize edge can be focused with the keyboard: then the arrow keys resize it and Home puts it back.

Card swipes ignore the outer edges and the bottom of each card, so a swipe meant for the screen is not taken by a card. Settings > General > Card Grid & Browse > Swipe gestures on character cards turns card swipes off, and Haptic feedback controls the small vibrations that confirm them.

Tips and tricks

The features that are easy to miss, each with a link to the section that covers it in full.

Finding and selecting

  • Prefixes and words mix in one search: creator:anon fav:yes elf finds your favorites by that creator with "elf" in the fields you search. Every prefix has to match, so two creator: prefixes find characters whose creator matches both. See Search prefixes.
  • Search for what is missing: linked:no finds characters linked to no site, playlist:none the ones in no playlist, and version:none the ones without a version. See Search prefixes.
  • Select All selects every character the view matches, not just the loaded cards. Click a few cards afterwards to leave them out, and the rest stay selected. See Selecting many characters.
  • A filter preset can open with the grid: pick it as the default under Settings > General > View Defaults. See The start page and view defaults.
  • Settings has its own search: type in the box at the top of the dialog to find a setting in any section. See Using Settings.

Characters and tags

  • In a character's details, ← and → step through the characters in the grid's own order, filters included. See Stepping to the next character.
  • Undo in the version history reverts the last restore whenever you come back to it, a reload later included. Its confirmation shows when the copy was taken, and anything you edited since the restore goes too. See Version history.
  • Import rules take regular expressions: a match written as /^(.+) \(OC\)$/ with the replacement $1 keeps the name and drops the suffix, and an empty replacement drops the tag. Rules run top to bottom and the first match wins; local files are never rewritten. See The Tags Manager.
  • Undo last bulk run also reverses a rename, a merge or a tag deleted everywhere, and the undo can itself be undone. See The Tags Manager.

Chatting

  • / jumps to the message box and End to the newest message; R, C, I and E regenerate, continue, write your next message and edit the last one. See A conversation.
  • Branch from here keeps more than the messages: the chat's own card texts, its Author's note and its persona come along, and with Memory or Trackers on, their state as of that message too. See Messages.
  • Combine alternates into a new one works on the opening message while it is still the last one, so the card's alternate greetings can be merged into a greeting of your own. See Combining alternates.
  • Inspect the prompt is a dry run of the next plain reply: a lorebook entry with a trigger chance is shown as if its roll passed, and what you have typed in the message box is not in it yet. See The prompt inspector.
  • Scrolling up while a reply is written stops the view from following it, so you can read back without fighting the stream. See While a reply is written.

Windows and the gallery

  • On a computer the conversation is a window too: drag its header to move it and its edges to resize it. Double-click a window's title bar, or the conversation's header, to put it back where it started. See How the windows work.
  • On a computer, a dialog's side panel (in the character details, Settings, the Lorebooks manager, the Custom CSS editor and the creator) can be widened: drag its inner edge, and double-click the edge to reset it. Reset Window Layouts puts every window back at once. See Scale, size and layout.
  • In a chat's Gallery window the mouse wheel zooms the picture, dragging moves a zoomed picture, and a click puts it back. See Gallery.
  • A chat can have a picture of its own behind it: Background in the chat's options menu, or Set as background on an image in its Gallery window. See Background.

Lorebooks, personas and the assistant

  • A lorebook bound to a persona follows you into every chat where you play that persona. See Where a lorebook applies.
  • The assistant's message edits can rewrite, replace, hide or delete a whole message, and a lorebook edit goes straight into the book. Applied changes have no undo, so read the side-by-side comparison first. See Proposed edits.
  • With Lore Search on, More Like This on a lorebook entry finds entries like it in every book, and Search Lore in More Options searches all of them at once. See Getting more from the bundled set.

Online and media

  • Auto-Link Characters looks at every unlinked character in your library, not just the view, and asks only the sites you have switched on that can search for a match. See Auto-Link Characters.
  • Download Media for All Characters leaves out the characters it has finished. Clear Processed History under Settings > Media lets the next run go over everything again. To fetch a few again, select them and press Media. See Downloading media for many characters.
  • Auto-Link Characters and Check All for Updates run in the tab that started them: keep it open until they finish. Media downloads run on the queue and pick up again after a reload.

Looks

  • A theme file carries your custom CSS too: Export saves the active CSS with the theme, and importing a theme replaces the active CSS when you save. See Themes, Custom CSS and theme extensions.
  • A token your custom CSS sets beats the same token in the theme; the theme editor marks such a setting Set in CSS. See How they stack.
  • The theme takes no pictures (a url( value is refused); a picture behind the whole app is a job for Custom CSS, and a picture behind one chat for its Background window. See Custom CSS.
  • clDisableCSS() in the browser's console switches custom CSS off for that tab until a reload, which tells you quickly whether your CSS is the cause of a problem. See Custom CSS.

Extensions

  • Chat Timeline opens from a chat's Timeline window, and from Chat Timeline in a character's card menu or a chat's menu in the Chats view. Double-click a point on it to open the chat there. See Getting more from the bundled set.
  • Swipes on the timeline draws each reply's unused swipes, and Take This Road starts a new chat from one of them. See Getting more from the bundled set.
  • On the timeline's map, / jumps to the search, the arrow keys walk from card to card, Enter opens the chat at the card, and Esc steps back (the style editor, the search, the card) before it closes the dialog.
  • Memory with Update after this many new messages at 0, and Trackers with Update after every reply off, update only when you ask, so they cost no model calls until then. See Getting more from the bundled set.
  • Translate into is only the default language: a chat can choose its own in its Translator window. See Getting more from the bundled set.

When something goes wrong

  • Starting the server with CL_EXTENSIONS=off loads the app with every extension switched off, so you can reach the one that broke it. See When an extension misbehaves.
  • After updating the server, reload the tabs you have open: a tab keeps the app it loaded, though it reconnects by itself after a restart. See Open tabs after an update.
  • ?server= with no value after the page's address forgets another server the page was pointed at. See Calling from another site.

The library

The Characters view is your library: every character you own, as a grid of cards. Searching and filtering it has its own page, Search and filters; one character's details are in Character details.

The grid

Each card shows the character's picture, its name, up to three tags, a gold star when it is a favorite, and a small badge when it belongs to a playlist (the playlist's own icon when it is in exactly one, a list icon otherwise; hover it for the names). Extensions can add chips of their own, such as a rating. On a computer, resting the pointer on a card shows a short excerpt of its creator's notes.

Click a card to open the character. The grid is one continuous scroll: it knows the size of the whole result at once and fetches cards as you reach them, so a library of any size scrolls the same way.

Display options, under Settings > General > Card Grid & Browse:

SettingDefaultWhat it does
Animate card info on hoveronHides the name and tags until you point at a card, then slides them up. Computers only.
Always show card nameoffWith the animation on, keeps the name visible and animates only the tags.
Hide playlist badges on cardsoffRemoves the playlist badge.
Prev/Next navigation in character detailsonLets you step through the grid from inside a character's details.

The card size follows the window and UI Scale (see Settings and themes). Which name a card shows follows Display name source (see Display names).

Sorting

The sort menu in the top bar orders the grid:

  • Name (A-Z) and Name (Z-A)
  • Last Modified (Newest) and Last Modified (Oldest)
  • Date Added (Newest) and Date Added (Oldest): when the character entered this library
  • Date Created (Newest) and Date Created (Oldest): the card's own creation date
  • Token Count (High to Low) and Token Count (Low to High): the estimated size of the card's text
  • Random: a fresh shuffle every time the grid reloads

Extensions can add sort orders of their own; each appears in a group named after its extension. The sort you pick lasts for the session; the one the library opens with is Settings > General > View Defaults > Characters > Sort, beside Group favorites first, which pins favorites to the top, sorted among themselves.

Favorites

Mark a favorite from the star in a character's details (Add to Favorites / Remove from Favorites), from its card menu, or for a whole selection with the Favorite button. On a phone, swipe a card to the right.

To see only favorites, tick Favorites only under Search Settings (the sliders beside the search box), type fav:yes, or add a Favorite rule in Advanced Filters.

Playlists

Playlists are named lists of characters, each with an optional icon and colour. A character can be in any number of them.

  • Adding: Add to Playlist in a card's menu, or Playlist in the selection toolbar, opens a picker. Type in Search or create... to find a playlist, or press Enter on a new name to create it. Each row's box shows whether all, some or none of the characters are in it; click to add, or to remove when all are already in.
  • Filtering: the Playlists button in the top bar shows All Characters and each playlist with its count; picking one filters the grid and the button takes its name. playlist: in the search box does the same (see Search prefixes).
  • Managing: Manage Playlists at the bottom of that menu, below.
  • In a character's details: on a computer, the sidebar lists the character's playlists. Click one to filter the grid by it, or use Add to playlist and Remove from playlist right there.
  1. Search or create...: finds a playlist; press Enter on a new name to create it.
  2. Change icon: the playlist's icon and colour.
  3. The playlist's name: click it to rename it.
  4. Delete playlist: the characters stay in your library.

The Tags Manager

More Options > Tags Manager works on tags across the whole library, in three tabs.

  1. The tabs: Library Tags, Batch Tagging and Import Rules, below.
  2. Search tags....
  3. Sorts the tags by name or by how many characters carry them.
  4. A tag's buttons, shown when you point at its row: Rename tag, Show in grid and Delete tag from all characters.

Library Tags lists every tag with how many characters carry it. Renaming a tag to a name that already exists merges the two; Show in grid closes the manager and filters the grid by that tag.

When tags differ only in capitals or spacing, a dupes chip offers Merge duplicates, with Merge or Merge + Add Import Rule (the second also maps the variants onto the kept spelling on future imports). Undo last bulk run reverses the most recent bulk tag change; it shows once there is one to undo.

Batch Tagging adds and removes tags on the characters you selected, or on every character in the current view.

Import Rules cleans tags as characters arrive from online sites: Strip emoji and decoration from imported tags, Clean whitespace on imported tags, Capitalize the first letter of imported tags, plus rules of your own that rewrite or drop a tag (Add Rule). Test a tag... shows what a rule set would make of a tag, and Apply to Library Now runs the rules over the tags you already have.

On a phone, tap a tag to see what you can do with it: Show in the Library, Rename (it asks for the new name) and Delete from Every Card. In Import Rules each rule is one line saying what it matches and what it becomes; tap it to edit it, to move it with Move Up and Move Down, or to Delete it, and tap Add a Rule at the end of the list for a new one. How Rules Work beside the first line explains the rest.

Tip

A rule's match can be a regular expression between slashes, and its replacement can use what it captured: /^(.+) \(OC\)$/ with the replacement $1 keeps the name and drops the suffix. An empty replacement drops the tag. Rules run top to bottom and the first match wins; local files are never rewritten. Undo last bulk run reverses a rename, a merge or a tag deleted everywhere too, and the undo can itself be undone.

Selecting many characters

Press Space on the Characters view (or pick Select for Batch from a card's menu) to start selecting; click cards to add or remove them. On a phone, use Select for Batch from a card's menu. Esc ends it.

  1. How many characters are selected.
  2. Select All.
  3. The actions, in the table below.
  4. Delete.
  5. Ends selecting, as Esc does.

The selection toolbar:

ButtonWhat it does
Select AllSelects every character the current search and filters match, however many
TagsOpens the Batch Tag Editor: Current Tags, Add Tags, Remove Tags, then Apply Changes
Favorite / UnfavoriteAdds them all to favorites, or removes them
ExportOpens the export chooser (see Exporting many characters)
UpdatesChecks the linked ones for updates on their sites (see Update checks)
MediaDownloads their remote media in the background (see Downloading media for many characters)
PlaylistAdds them to a playlist
MoreHolds what does not fit, including actions extensions add
DeleteDeletes them, after a confirmation

Select All selects the view itself, not just the cards loaded so far: a search matching 40,000 characters selects all 40,000, and the toolbar's count comes from the server. Changing the search or filters clears the selection, so an action never lands on a set you have stopped looking at.

Tip

To act on all but a few, press Select All and then click the cards to leave out. The rest of the view stays selected, and the count follows.

A change to a large set runs on the server as a background job, a chunk at a time. Tag changes show their progress on the Apply Changes button and can be stopped with Cancel; what already landed stays. Every bulk tag change is journaled, so the Tags Manager's Undo last bulk run can reverse it. Deleting, favoriting and playlist changes report when they finish.

Deleting a selection asks first, names the first few characters, and says that each goes with its chats, gallery files, snapshots and provider links. If no search or filter is active, it warns that the selection is every character in your library. A delete over a filtered set is refused if the set changed since it was counted, and asks again.

The card menu

Right-click a card (on a phone, long-press it or swipe it left) for its menu:

  • Open Character
  • Add to Favorites / Remove from Favorites
  • Select for Batch / Deselect
  • Link to Provider, or for a linked character its site's info and Check for Updates (see Linking characters to their source)
  • Version History (see Version history)
  • View Gallery
  • Download Media (see Downloading a card's remote media)
  • Add to Playlist
  • Export Character
  • actions added by extensions
  • Delete Character

With several characters selected, right-clicking one of them shows the selection's menu instead: Edit Tags, Check for Updates, Download Media, Add to Playlist, Add All to Favorites, Remove All from Favorites, Export All, Delete All and Clear Selection.

Finding duplicates

More Options > Find Duplicates looks for characters that are probably the same card: names that match or nearly match, and the same card under a new name (its description and first message unchanged). It compares the creator, the creator's notes, the description, the first message, the personality and the scenario, and treats two characters linked to the same online page as the same, and two linked to different pages on the same site as different when their text differs; one the text cannot confirm is shown with that reason. Characters that only share a name, by different creators with different stories, are never paired. Each pair shows a Keep card beside a Potential Duplicate, with the fields that differ, side by side with every changed word marked, and the ones that match, rated from Possible Match through Similar, Very Similar and Near-Identical to Identical. Each card has View, Playlist and Delete; nothing is merged.

Settings > General > Duplicate Detection > Minimum Match Score (20 to 120, default 35) sets how close a pair must be to show; lower shows more. At 120 the readout says Exact and only identical cards show, with an Add to Playlist button that collects all the newer or all the older copies at once.

Deleting from here is the same as deleting anywhere: the character goes with its chats and gallery.

The Card Recommender

More Options > Card Recommender asks one of your AI connections to pick characters from your own library. Type what you are in the mood for and press Recommend. The recommender draws a random sample of your library, sends each sampled character's name and a few short details to the model, and shows its picks with a reason for each.

  1. What you are in the mood for.
  2. Recommend.
  3. The connection and its Overrides.
  4. Settings, below.
  5. Add all to playlist.
  6. Reroll: asks again with the same request, leaving out what was already suggested.
  7. A pick's Add to playlist and Ask about this character, below.

The Settings button (the sliders) holds three groups:

  • Sample Pool: which characters can be sampled: Has Chats and Favorite (each Yes, Any or No), a Date Created range (tick Enable), and Include Tags / Exclude Tags. A line shows how many characters match.
  • LLM Context: what is sent about each character: tags, creator's notes, tagline, creator (on by default), description and source site (off by default). An estimate shows the request's size. Text is cleaned and cut short before sending; your chats are never sent.
  • Generation: Sample (how many characters are sent, default 100), Max (how many picks, default 10), Batch Mode (splits a larger sample across several requests and ranks the results in a final one), and Batches (3 to 7).

The connection and its overrides sit at the top (see Connections). Settings are remembered when you press Recommend.

Each result shows the character, its reason (click to read it all) and its tags; click the name to open the character.

Ask about this character on a result opens a small chat about that one card: the model reads its full text (and its lorebook, when you tick Include lorebook) and answers your questions.

  1. Chat settings: shows the system prompt, which you can edit.
  2. Load a Prompt...: a prompt you saved.
  3. Save current as a preset, Delete the selected preset and Reset to the default prompt.
  4. Your question.
  5. Send.

Notifications

The bell in the top bar appears only when there is something to show: a download running, an import working in the background, a backup, an extension's job, the search index being tidied, an extension update, or a failure. It shows a working icon while busy and a warning badge when something failed. On a phone it is Notifications in the More sheet, and the More button wears a dot while something runs, something failed or an extension update waits.

Its sections:

  • Media Downloads: the background media queue (see Background downloads), with Cancel this download, Retry and Dismiss for failures, and Clear finished.
  • Imports: characters importing in the background from the Online view, with Retry, Download media and Dismiss.
  • Library Backup: a backup copying, with its progress and Cancel the backup; a backup that failed, with Dismiss; and a scheduled backup that did not run, asking Back Up Now, Tonight or Skip (see Library Backup).
  • Extension Jobs: long work an extension runs, such as tagging characters with AI, with Cancel this job.
  • Maintenance: shown while the app tidies its search index in the background, which it does when the app sits idle and an index is in pieces, or when you ask in Search Index, naming the part it is working on (card text, lorebook entries or chat messages). Until it finishes, searches and the Related tab may be slower and the disk busier. It needs nothing from you and leaves on its own (see Keeping it that fast). Opening the notifications to read it, or coming back to the app, leaves it running; one the app started while it sat idle pauses at your next click, key or scroll elsewhere in the app.
  • Extension updates: for an admin who switched on Tell me when an extension has an update, the installed extensions with a newer version, each with the version you have and the one coming, and Open Extensions, which opens Settings at the first of them (see Keeping installed extensions up to date).

Extensions can add sections of their own. When nothing is going on the bell hides.

Search and filters

Search Everything finds anything in the app; everything after it on this page narrows the Characters grid, and the search box, the tag and playlist filters and Advanced Filters all combine: a character shows only when it passes every one of them. The server runs the whole query, so a search over a huge library costs about what it costs over a small one.

Search everything

Search Everything is one field for the whole app. Press the key under Esc, left of the 1 (` on a US keyboard, ^ on a German one, § on a Nordic one), anywhere outside a text field, or pick Search Everything at the top of More Options (on a phone, in the More menu).

Type a few letters and it finds, grouped and best first:

  • your characters, with their portraits: by name, nickname and creator first, then by what their cards say (description, first message, personality, scenario, creator's notes, example messages, alternate greetings and the rest of their text);
  • your chats, by title or by character;
  • the messages in your chats that hold every word you typed (a single letter on its own does not count as a word), each shown with the words marked;
  • your lorebooks, by name;
  • the lorebook entries whose title or content hold every word you typed, each shown with its lorebook and the words marked;
  • your tags, playlists and personas;
  • every setting, by its label;
  • the views, the More Options items and every extension command that needs no input.

When a group holds more than it lists, its heading says Show all: click or tap it to see every result of that kind for what you typed, more loading as you scroll. Back, Esc or the phone's back button returns to all your results.

While a search is still running, an empty list says Searching; it says Nothing matches only once every part of the search has answered, and Could not search when part of it failed (try again).

The best match of all leads as the Top hit. On a wide window a preview beside the list shows the highlighted character's portrait, tags and notes, a chat's last message, the matched message, or the matched entry's text.

Enter does the obvious thing: it opens a character's details, opens a chat (a message's chat at that message), opens a lorebook in the Lorebooks manager (an entry's lorebook with that entry open and marked), shows a tag's or a playlist's characters, makes a persona yours, or opens Settings at a setting with the settings search already set to it.

Tab (or a right-click; on a phone, the ⋯ at the end of a row) opens what else you can do with a result. For a character: Open, Chat (their latest chat, or their first one), Add to favorites or Remove from favorites, Add a tag, Add to a playlist, Check for updates (for a character linked to a site) and Delete (which asks first). For a chat: open it, or open its character. For a tag: show its characters, or add it to the characters you have selected. For a lorebook entry: open it, or open its lorebook. For a persona: use it, or edit it in Settings.

Some actions ask for what they act on in the field itself. Chat with…, Open a lorebook…, Show characters tagged… and Search the library for… show their name at the start of the field; type, and pick from what it finds. Add a tag offers your tags and, when you type a new one, adds that. With characters selected, Add a tag to the selection… tags them all at once.

Esc (or the phone's back button, or Back) steps out of an action to your search, and closes the field from there. With nothing typed, the field leads with what you last opened from it, then those actions and the views.

Inside cards, messages and lorebook entries, a word is found when it starts with what you typed ("drag" finds "dragon"), and the search starts at the third letter. Each can be switched off under Settings > General > Search Everything: Search inside character cards, Search inside chat messages and Search inside lorebooks. All three are on to begin with; names, chats, lorebook names and the rest are always searched.

The search box

Type in the search box at the top. The grid follows as you type, waiting a moment between keystrokes (a little longer for one or two characters). The × in the box clears it.

What plain text matches is set by Search Settings, the sliders button beside the box:

Under Search inDefaultMatches
Character NameonThe card's name and its nickname
Listing NameonThe title the character has on its online page
TagsonAny tag containing the text
AuthoroffThe card's creator
Creator's NotesoffThe creator's notes, word by word (see below)
TaglineoffThe short tagline
Under Card text
DescriptionoffThe card's description, word by word
First MessageoffThe first message, word by word
Alt GreetingsoffEvery alternate greeting, word by word

Favorites only at the bottom of the same menu shows favorites alone.

The name, listing name, tags, author and tagline match what you type as one piece of text, anywhere inside the field, ignoring capitals.

Creator's notes and the card text are searched word by word, and search finds text inside words: every word you type must appear inside some word of the field, so agon finds "dragon" and witch finds "bewitched". Capitals and accents are ignored (cafe finds "café"). Typing several words finds the characters that have all of them, in any order and anywhere in the ticked fields. Put words in quotes to find them as a phrase: "red dragon" finds those two whole words side by side, in that order. Punctuation only separates words, so a search of punctuation alone finds no card text.

Card text is searched through an index, so it answers quickly at any library size. A word of one or two letters is the exception: it sits inside so many words that the search reads every card instead, which is slower.

With text typed and no field ticked, nothing matches.

These boxes reset when you reload; the ones the library starts with are Settings > General > Search Defaults, where the creator's notes box is called Creator Notes.

On a phone, the floating search button opens the search, and the sliders button in the bottom bar holds the sort, the favorites, tags and playlists filters, and a shorter Search in list.

Search prefixes

Prefixes filter by one property each. Type a prefix and its value with no space after the colon, at the start of the box or after a space. Several prefixes combine (a character must match all of them), and whatever text is left over is searched as above. Prefix names and values ignore capitals. A value runs to the next space, so it cannot contain one; use Advanced Filters for a multi-word value.

Tip

Prefixes find what is missing as well as what is there: linked:no lists characters linked to no site, playlist:none the ones in no playlist, and version:none the ones without a version.

PrefixValuesShows
creator:textCharacters whose creator contains the text
favorite: or fav:yes or noFavorites, or the rest
linked:yes or noCharacters linked to any online site, or to none
version:text, or noneCharacters whose version contains the text, or that have none
uid:part of an idThe character with that id (the Info tab shows it)
playlist:a name, any or noneCharacters in a playlist whose name contains the text, in any playlist, or in none
chub:yes or noLinked to ChubAI, or not
janitorai: or jai:yes or noLinked to JanitorAI, or not
janny:yes or noLinked to JannyAI, or not
charactertavern: or ct:yes or noLinked to CharacterTavern, or not
pygmalion:yes or noLinked to Pygmalion, or not
wyvern:yes or noLinked to Wyvern, or not
datacat: or dc:yes or noLinked to DataCat, or not
botbooru: or bb:yes or noLinked to Botbooru, or not
risu:yes or noLinked to the RisuAI Realm, or not

For the yes-or-no prefixes, yes (or true) means yes and any other value means no. Extensions that add an online site add its prefix too.

An example: creator:ann fav:yes chub:no elf shows favorites by a creator whose name contains "ann", not linked to ChubAI, matching "elf" in the fields ticked under Search in.

The tag filter

Tags in the top bar opens the tag list, with Find tags... to search it and a button to sort by name or by how many characters carry each tag. Click a tag (or the button beside it) to cycle it:

  • Included (a tick): only characters with this tag.
  • Excluded (a minus): characters with this tag are hidden.
  • Neutral: no effect.

Once a tag is active, two switches appear above the list:

  • Match Any, which a click turns to Match All: whether a character needs any one of the included tags, or all of them.
  • Hide All, which a click turns to Hide Any: with Hide All (the default) a character is hidden only when it carries every excluded tag; with Hide Any, one excluded tag is enough.

Both choices are remembered. The Tags button shows the active counts, and Clear all tag filters (the ×) resets them. Tags you have activated stay at the top of the list.

The playlist filter

Playlists in the top bar filters the grid to one playlist; All Characters goes back to everything. See Playlists.

Advanced Filters

The funnel button () in the search box opens Advanced Filters: rules built from a field, an operator and a value, all of which must hold.

  1. Presets: saved sets of rules, below.
  2. Clear All: empties the panel.
  3. Filter or name new preset... and its save button. See Filter presets.
  4. A rule: its field, its operator and its value.
  5. Removes the rule.
  6. Add Filter: adds a rule.
FieldOperators
Name, Nickname, Listing Name, Creatorcontains, does not contain, equals, starts with, is empty, is not empty
Tagsincludes, excludes
Creator Notes, Description, Personality, Scenario, First Message, Example Dialogue, System Prompt Text, Post-History Instructions, Character's Notecontains, does not contain, is empty, is not empty
Favoriteyes, no
Provider Linkis linked, is not linked, linked to (a site), not linked to (a site)
Linked Lorebookis linked, is not linked, contains, does not contain, equals
Date Added, Date Created, Date Modifiedbefore, after (a date), in the last (a number of days)
Last Chatbefore, after, in the last, never
Versioncontains, is empty, is not empty
Token Countmore than, less than, equals
Playlistin, not in, in any, not in any
Name Overrideyes, no, to card name, to listing name
Embedded Lorebookyes, no
Alt Greetingsmore than, less than, equals (a count)
Card Spec (e.g. 3.0)equals

Extensions can add fields of their own (the Ratings and Notes extension adds Rating), grouped under the extension's name, with is set and is not set besides their own operators.

Filter presets

Presets at the top of the panel saves and loads sets of Advanced Filter rules. Type a name in Filter or name new preset... (up to 40 characters) and press Enter or the save button; the same box filters the list when you have many. Click a preset to load it, or its × to delete it. A preset holds the Advanced Filter rules only, not the search text, tags or sort.

The Characters and Chats views keep separate preset lists. To open the library with a preset applied, pick it in Settings > General > View Defaults > Characters > Filter preset.

Searching chats

In the Chats view the search box matches the chat's name and the character's name. To search inside messages, use Advanced Filters, which has its own fields there:

FieldOperators
Character, Chat Namecontains, does not contain, equals, starts with
Messagesmore than, less than, equals (a count)
Message Textcontains, does not contain
Last Messagebefore, after, in the last
Char Favoriteyes, no
Char Tagsincludes, excludes
Char Provider Linkis linked, is not linked, linked to, not linked to
Char Playlistin, not in, in any, not in any
Chat Lorebookis not empty, is empty, contains, equals

Message Text searches by words: every word you type must appear in the same message, each matching the start of a word, ignoring capitals and accents. A single letter on its own is passed over, while a number and a single Chinese, Japanese or Korean character count, matching the words that start with them.

Searching online

Each online site has its own search box, sorts and filters in the Online view. See Browsing online.

Built for speed

Project Σ is fast. Really fast. Here are typical answer times on a real library of 17,417 characters, with 292 chats holding 25,917 messages, 178 lorebooks holding 3,202 entries, and 13,560 tags, on an everyday desktop PC:

What you askAnswered in
Search Everything: characters, card text, chats, every message, lorebooks, every entry and tags, all at onceabout 10 ms
Every card whose text mentions "forest" (2,175 of them)7 ms
Every chat message holding a word1 to 3 ms
The search box, "el" across names and tags (2,697 matches)14 ms
An Advanced Filters rule looking for a name anywhere in Description29 ms
Related Characters for a cardabout 35 ms

A blink of an eye takes 100 to 400 ms. Every one of these answers comes back well before you finish blinking.

Keeping it that fast

To stay this quick, Project Σ looks after its search index on its own. Once a big import, a SillyTavern migration, an update or everyday edits have left it in pieces, it tidies the index in the background the next time the app sits idle for two minutes, and pauses the moment you click, type or scroll again, so you never wait on it. A tidy-up takes seconds on a small library and can take several minutes for tens of thousands of cards on a hard drive; it picks up where it left off until it is done. The bell tells you while it runs, so you always know what is going on behind the scenes:

There is nothing to do and nothing to wait for. The row leaves on its own when the tidy-up is done. To see how tidy each index is, or to tidy it yourself, open Search Index in the settings.

Character details

Click a card in the grid (or pick Open Character from its menu) to open the character's details. The window opens on the Details tab and fills in as the card's text arrives.

The details window

  1. Toggle Favorite: adds the character to your favorites, or takes it off.
  2. Delete Character. See Deleting a character.
  3. Chat: resumes the character's latest conversation, or starts its first.
  4. The picture: click it to view it full size, with the gallery after it.
  5. The tags; a +N pill shows the rest.
  6. The character's playlists; Add to playlist puts it in another.
  7. The tabs: Details, Edit, Chats, Gallery and Related, plus Info when it is switched on and any tabs extensions add.

On a narrow window, the tabs that do not fit wait behind More tabs. On a phone the sidebar (4 to 6) folds away: tap the round picture in the header to view it, the tags move into the Edit tab, and you swipe the tab content left or right to change tab.

Stepping to the next character

With Prev/Next navigation in character details on (the default), you can move through the grid without closing the window: the chevrons at its sides on a computer, ← and → on the keyboard, or a swipe across the header on a phone. The order is the grid's current sort and filters. If you have unsaved edits, you are asked first.

The Details tab

The line under the header shows:

  • Created, the date the character entered your library (or the card's own date when that is unknown).
  • Author, the card's creator: click it to search the library for that creator.
  • The link chip: Link when the character is not linked to an online site (click to link it), or the site's name when it is, with a lock when update checks are switched off for it. See Linking characters to their source.
  • The name toggle (see Display names).

Below come the site's tagline (when Show provider tagline in character details is on), Creator's Notes, Description, First Message, Alternate Greetings, the card's own lorebook (Embedded Lorebook), and the lorebooks linked to the character (Linked Lorebooks). Click a title to open long text full screen; the creator's notes and the greetings also have zoom controls there.

Creator's notes are often written in HTML with their own styling. With Rich creator's notes rendering on (the default) they render in a sealed frame that keeps their styling away from the app; off, they show as plain formatted text. Expand creator's notes by default opens them on every character. Both are under Settings > General > Character Details, with Allow rich tagline display for HTML taglines.

When media localization is on, links in the card text to images you have downloaded show your local copies (see Media).

Display names

A character can have up to three names: the card's own name, a listing name (the title on its online page) and a nickname you give it. Settings > General > Display Names > Display name source picks which one the grid and the details show (Card Name, Listing Name or Nickname), falling back to the card name when the chosen one is empty. Nothing about the card changes; it is only what you see.

For one character, the name toggle on the Details tab (Card, Listing or Nickname) cycles through the names it has and remembers your choice for that character. It shows when the character has at least two different names and Show name type toggle in details is on. The Name Override rule in Advanced Filters finds the characters you set this way.

Editing

The Edit tab opens locked, so nothing changes by accident. Press Unlock Editing to edit; Lock Editing locks it again. Fields, in the tab's groups:

GroupFields
Basic InformationCharacter Name, Creator / Author, Character Version, Tagline, Nickname, Listing Name
Character DefinitionDescription / Persona, Personality Summary, Scenario / Setting
MessagesFirst Message, the alternate greetings (one box each, Add Greeting for more), Example Dialogue (for training context)
Advanced (Prompt Overrides)System Prompt (Main Prompt Override), Post-History Instructions (Jailbreak), the Character's Note with its depth and role, Creator's Notes (For users, not sent to AI)

The Character's Note is a standing reminder sent inside the chat, a few messages from the end: Note Depth is how many turns back (4 when left blank; a depth below 0 sends nothing) and Note Sent As is the role it speaks with. An empty note removes it.

More ways to edit:

  • Expand all fields to fit content grows every box to its text.
  • Expand editor on a long field opens it full screen, with Apply to bring the text back. Creator's notes there have a preview.
  • Edit all greetings in expanded view puts the first message and every alternate greeting in one window, with Collapse All, Expand All, Add Greeting and Apply All.
  • Tags: on a computer, type in Add tag... in the sidebar (existing tags are suggested, and Create makes a new one); the × on a tag removes it. On a phone, Edit Tags opens a sheet.
  • The picture: with editing unlocked, click the portrait (Change Image), or tap the header picture on a phone, and pick a PNG, JPEG or WebP up to 10 MB. It shows as pending until you save; the × reverts it. Saving converts it to PNG; picking a card PNG as the picture changes only the picture, never the card's text.
  • Lorebooks: the Linked Lorebooks and Embedded Lorebook sections are described in Lorebooks. Linking and unlinking a lorebook happens at once; edits to the embedded book land with Save Changes.

Saving

Save Changes shows every changed field, old above new with the change highlighted, and Confirm Save writes them. With Auto-snapshot before edits and updates on (the default), the character as it was is saved to its version history first (see Version history), so a save can be undone. Cancel throws your edits away.

Closing the window, stepping to another character, or pressing Esc with unsaved edits asks first (Discard or Keep Editing).

The Chats tab

Every chat with this character: its name, when it was last active, its message count, and the lorebooks bound to it. Click a chat to open it. New Chat starts a fresh one. The row buttons bind lorebooks to a chat, open it, or delete it. See Chats.

The Gallery tab

The character's images, videos and audio. Drop files onto Drop files here to add to gallery (or click it) to add them, and press Download Media to fetch the remote images the card links to. See Media and galleries.

The Related tab

Related Characters lists characters that may share a universe or themes with this one, strongest first. Three switches choose what counts:

  • Shared Tags: tags in common, a rare tag counting for much more than a common one. A tag most of your library carries is ignored, and spellings that differ only in case, spaces, dashes or underscores count as one tag.
  • Same Creator: a character by the same creator gets a small lift, more when that creator has few characters. At most five characters by one creator are listed, so a prolific creator never fills the tab.
  • Content Similarity: the rare words both characters use in their description, personality, scenario and first message, such as names and places. A word much of your library uses does not count, so this reads best in a larger library.

Results are grouped as strongly, moderately and possibly related, each with its reasons ("Both mention Hyrule, Sheikah", the shared tags, the same creator). Copies of one character show once, with a Copies button that counts every copy and opens a list of up to eleven of them (the one shown and ten more), each with its creator, when it was added and its length: pick one to open it. Two characters count as copies when the duplicate check would call them the same character, never just for sharing a name. Click a result to open it in place.

Version history

Every character keeps a history of snapshots: copies of the whole card as it was at a moment. Open it with Versions next to Unlock Editing on the Edit tab, Version History in the card menu, or Version History in the link window.

Snapshots are saved:

  • automatically before an edit, an update from the character's online page, linking and unlinking, while Auto-snapshot before edits and updates is on (Settings > General > Version History, on by default), and before every restore whatever the setting;
  • whenever you press Save Snapshot and give it a label.

An automatic snapshot that cannot be saved never holds the change up: the change goes ahead, and a warning names the character that was changed without one (one warning for a whole batch).

Automatic snapshots are capped per character by Max auto-backup snapshots per character (10 by default, 0 for no limit); the oldest automatic ones go first, and ones you saved by hand are never removed. An automatic snapshot that would be identical to the previous one is skipped.

The Chat Assistant saves the card before each change it writes, as a snapshot marked with a wand. These keep a limit of their own, Max Chat Assistant snapshots per character (10 by default, 0 for no limit), so a long session with the assistant never pushes out your other automatic snapshots, and edits of your own never push out the assistant's.

Click a snapshot to compare it with the character as it is now: every field that differs, long text as a line-by-line diff, tags as added and removed, greetings one by one, the embedded lorebook entry by entry, and the picture (with Apply Avatar to take just the picture back). Snapshots you saved can be renamed and deleted.

Restore puts the character back to that snapshot. The state before the restore is kept with the character until the next restore, so Undo reverts it whenever you come back, after a reload too; its confirmation shows when that copy was taken, and anything you edited since goes with it. Restoring changes the card's text and its embedded lorebook, not its picture or its linked lorebooks.

For a character linked to ChubAI, the history has a second tab, Remote, listing every published version of the card on ChubAI (and the page's current state), which you can compare and restore the same way. When the page's linked lorebook cannot be read from ChubAI, its comparison says so and shows no lorebook difference, and restoring it keeps the character's lorebook as it is. No other site publishes version history.

The Info tab

Switch on Show "Info" tab in character modal under Settings > Developer to see an Info tab with the card's technical details: its id (what uid: searches), its link, its lorebooks, media counts, token estimate, card spec, dates, the data extensions stored on it, and Copy Raw Card Data.

Deleting a character

The bin in the header, or Delete Character in the card menu, deletes a character after a confirmation that counts what goes with it; a count it could not read is said, never left out. Deleting removes the character together with its chats, its gallery files, its version snapshots and its links to playlists, tags and lorebooks. The lorebooks and playlists themselves stay. There is no undo, so export the character first if you might want it back.

When it changes somewhere else

If the open character is saved in another tab or on another device, a Changed elsewhere band appears with Reload. If it was deleted elsewhere, the window says so and closes.

Importing and exporting

Import Characters

More Options > Import Characters (on the Characters view) brings characters in from links or files. Pick the source at the top:

  • URLs: paste one character page link per line, from any online site you have switched on (see Browsing online). Each link is fetched from its site and imported with its link kept, so update checks work later.
  • Local Files: drop files onto Drag & drop card or bundle files here, or press Browse Files. Accepted:
    • card PNGs (V1, V2 and V3 cards, up to 100 MB). When a card carries both a V2 and a V3 copy of itself, the V3 copy is the one imported, and anything only the V2 copy holds is kept too.
