A RisuAI card's packed-in images (expressions, backgrounds) go into the character's gallery, and stay inside the card's picture, so an exported card still carries them back to RisuAI;
    • CHARX files: the card, its main picture, and its other images, which go into the character's gallery;
    • a Project Σ bundle (.zip), which opens the bundle review instead (see Importing a bundle).

Options under the source:

OptionDefaultWhat it does
Skip duplicates (already in library)onSkips a link to a page one of your characters is already linked to, or listed twice
Auto-download galleryfollows Include Provider GalleryDownloads the gallery from the character's site after importing
Auto-download embedded mediafollows Replace remote URLs with local filesDownloads the images the card links to (see Media)

Press Import. A progress bar and counts of imported, skipped and failed cards follow, and a log lists each one. When a file matches a character you have but its content differs, you are asked whether it is a new copy (Import as Copy) or not (Skip). Closing the dialog during an import asks whether to stop.

If the duplicate check itself cannot run (the server could not answer it), the import stops to ask once: Import Without Checking brings in this card and the rest of the run unchecked, each marked "not checked for duplicates" in the log; Skip Them skips every card the check could not look at, with the reason in the log.

Imports never overwrite a character you already have. To replace one deliberately, use Replace with This Card when an online import warns of a duplicate (below), or restore an older version from Version history.

JSON card files are not accepted; a card JSON's lorebook can be imported on its own in the Lorebooks manager.

Links that are not character pages

A link that no site recognizes (a file link on an image host or a chat attachment, say) is skipped, with a note pointing at Settings > General > Imports > Import unrecognized URLs as direct downloads. With that on, such a link is downloaded as a file and imported if it is a card PNG or CHARX. Links to private or local network addresses are never fetched.

Tags on import

Tags on characters imported from online sites pass through the Tags Manager's import rules first (see The Tags Manager).

The duplicate warning

Importing from an online preview first looks for the character in your library: by its online page, by its name (every character of that name, however common), by its creator, and by its text, so the same card under a new name is found too. A character you own from another page on the same site is compared by its text: a different card by the same creator is left out, and one the text cannot confirm (a hidden definition, say) is shown with that reason. Matches below the minimum score of Find Duplicates are left out once their text has been compared, so the two use one setting; a character linked to that very page, and a match with no text to compare, are always shown. A card wearing a Possible Match badge always gets the window, even when the full check finds nothing closer. When something similar is there, a Potential Duplicate Found window shows the matches, each rated as in Find Duplicates with the reason, with:

  • Skip Import: nothing happens.
  • Import Anyway: the card comes in as a separate character.
  • Replace with This Card: the character you pick keeps its chats, tags, favorite, gallery and anything extensions keep on it; its card text and picture (embedded lorebook included) become the new card's. The card it had is saved in its version history first. If the new card carries different regex scripts than the ones you allowed, they stay off until you allow them again.

If the check cannot run, a Duplicate check failed window gives the reason and asks before anything comes in: Import Anyway or Cancel.

Exporting one character

Export Character in a card's menu downloads the character as a card PNG. The file carries the card's text, its tags, its embedded lorebook, its link to its online page, the name of its first linked lorebook, and an id that lets this app recognize it again. It does not carry favorites, chats, the gallery or further linked lorebooks; a bundle does (below).

With Settings > Developer > Export Character downloads set to CHARX (Card and Gallery), Export Character downloads a CHARX file instead: the card as a V3 card, its picture, and every file in its gallery (images, videos and audio), which apps that read V3 cards open with the gallery. The card in your library does not change. Importing your own CHARX back gives the card exactly as it was, so it is recognized as already in your library; if another app has edited the file since, it comes in as the V3 card it became.

With Settings > Developer > Export as provider links on, Export Character copies the character's online page link to the clipboard instead of downloading a file. Characters with no link are skipped.

Exporting many characters

Select characters (see Selecting many characters) and press Export in the toolbar. The chooser offers:

  • Full Bundle (.zip): cards, every chat and every gallery file in one archive, for moving characters to another Project Σ library. With Include linked lorebooks in the full bundle (on by default) their lorebooks travel too.
  • Character Cards (.png): one PNG per character, as above.
  • Character Cards with Gallery (.charx): one CHARX per character, as above, each with its gallery.
  • Provider Links: the online page links of the linked characters, copied to the clipboard.

A progress bar follows, with Cancel. Bundles can be large: everything is packed on the server first, then downloaded. Export All in the selection's right-click menu skips the chooser and exports the card files Export Character downloads picks (or links, with Export as provider links on).

A bundle holds the cards, the chats (as SillyTavern-style JSONL files), the gallery files and the lorebooks. It does not hold version history, playlists, personas, settings, connections, prompt presets, chat backgrounds, or data that extensions keep (ratings, notes, trackers and the like).

To back up a whole library, use Library Backup and copy the data/ folder; a bundle is for moving characters, not a full backup.

Importing a bundle

Drop a bundle .zip into Import Characters > Local Files. The Import Bundle review lists every character in it (those you already have are marked In library), with how many chats and gallery files each brings, and a box for the bundled lorebooks. Untick what you do not want, then press Import.

A bundle never overwrites:

  • A character identical to one you have is reused, and the bundle's new chats and gallery files attach to it.
  • A character that has changed since (a different version of one you have) is skipped and listed. Afterwards, a button imports those as copies if you want them.
  • A bundled lorebook identical to one you have is reused; a different book with the same name comes in as "Name (2)".

Each character lands completely or not at all. Bundles made by the Character Library extension for SillyTavern are refused; bring that data across with the migration instead.

Chats

Single chat files cannot be imported through the app. Chats arrive inside bundles and through the SillyTavern migration. Each chat exports on its own as JSONL from the conversation's options menu (see Exporting a chat).

Creating characters

More Options > Create Character opens the character creator: a form for a new card, and the Studio, an AI design partner that talks the whole draft through with you. The Studio needs a connection (see Connections); the form does not.

The form

On a computer the picture and tags sit on the left and the fields on the right; on a phone they stack, with Import and Studio buttons at the top.

  1. Opens the Studio. See The Studio.
  2. Expand all fields to fit content: grows every box to its text.
  3. Click or drop an image: any common image type, up to 20 MB. Without one, the card gets a plain placeholder.
  4. Suggest tags from the draft: asks your connection for tags that fit the draft and shows them as Suggestions, with Add All.
  5. Add tag...: your library's tags are suggested as soon as you click in and narrow as you type; click one (or light it with the arrow keys and press Enter) to add it, or press Enter to add what you typed.
  6. Work on this field in the studio, beside each field. See Working on one field.
  7. Import from Library. See Starting from a character you have.
  8. Create Character.
  9. The other ways to save. See Saving.
  • The fields: Character Name (the only required one), Creator / Author, Version, Tagline, Description / Persona, Personality Summary, Scenario / Setting, First Message, Alternate Greetings (with Add Greeting), Example Dialogue, System Prompt (Override), Post-History Instructions (Jailbreak) and Creator's Notes (Not sent to AI), which has a rendered preview.

The creator has no lorebook, nickname or Character's Note fields; add those in the character's details after creating it.

Starting from a character you have

Import from Library loads an existing character into the form: its text, tagline, greetings, tags and picture. From there you can make a variation as a new character, or save your changes back over the original. If its picture could not be loaded, a warning says so and the form is left without one: add a picture before Create Character, or the character is saved without one.

When you then press Create Character, a Carried Over dialog lists what the imported character has that the form has no fields for: its embedded lorebook, its Character's Note, its other card settings and its gallery, each with a switch, all on. Create Character there gives the new character the parts left on, and copies the gallery's files into the new character's own gallery; Back returns to the form without creating anything. Its link to a character site and its linked lorebooks stay with the original. Saving over a character never carries these parts.

Saving

  • Create Character always creates a new character, then opens its details.
  • The arrow beside it saves over an existing character instead: Save Over the character you imported from, or Save as Existing Character... to pick any character. Either shows Review Changes, every field that will change, before Save Changes. With Auto-snapshot before edits and updates on, the character's previous state is saved to its version history first.

Closing the creator with work in it asks first. The form itself is not saved between visits, but a Studio conversation keeps the draft it was working on (below).

The Studio

Open it with the wand at the top of the creator (on a phone, Studio). It is one conversation about the whole card: you talk about who the character is, and it reads the current draft every time you send.

  1. The wand at the top opens and closes the Studio.
  2. Work on this field in the studio, beside each field. See Working on one field.
  3. Session: the Studio conversations about this draft. See Sessions.
  4. New conversation, Rename this conversation and Delete this conversation.
  5. What it can see: the draft and the reference cards it is sent. See What the Studio sees.
  6. Studio settings. See Studio settings.
  7. A proposed change's own buttons: Show the changes side by side, Touch up the text before applying, Apply this change now and Set this change aside. See Proposed changes.
  8. Apply Selected: applies the ticked changes to the form.
  9. What your next message is about: Whole Card, or the field you are working on; and Add reference cards.
  10. Saved prompts: picking one fills the box, it does not send.
  11. Attach pictures, below.
  12. Send.

When the conversation is empty it offers starting points, such as fleshing out a rough idea or drafting the card from what you have. Type in Talk about the character... and send with Enter (on a phone, use the send button). The saved prompts start with Draft the card, Suggest tags and Three greetings, and hold any you add.

The paperclip beside Send shows the Studio pictures, for example to write the appearance from a portrait or to build a character from reference art: Pictures from this device, or The draft's picture (the one in the form, saved or not). You can also paste or drop pictures. They are kept with the Studio conversation and deleted with it. The paperclip shows only when the Studio's connection is set as seeing pictures.

Working on one field

Each field in the form has a wand (Work on this field in the studio). Clicking it points your next message at that field. Select part of a field's text first and the next message revises just that passage.

Proposed changes

When the Studio suggests text for the card, it arrives as proposed changes under Proposed changes, not as edits already made. Each change says what it does (writes a field, replaces a passage, changes tags, adds a greeting). For each you can compare Now and Proposed side by side, touch the text up, apply it on its own, or set it aside. Apply Selected applies the ticked ones. Applying changes the form only; nothing is saved to your library until you press Create Character or save over a character.

A change that cannot be applied as written (marked in yellow or red, with the reason) offers Try again : the Studio is told what went wrong, re-reads the draft as it is now and proposes the change again, or says it does not fit any more. A reply with several such changes also offers Try All Again in its footer. Once the Studio has replied again, it is not shown the changes of an earlier reply you left unapplied, so it only builds on what is in the form.

Every message can be copied, edited (Edit this message), asked again from that point (Ask again from here), or deleted.

Sessions

Studio conversations are saved on the server with the draft they were about. The session menu at the top switches between them, starts a New Conversation, renames or deletes one. Switching to a session whose draft differs from the form asks Restore this conversation's draft? When you create the character or save over one, its sessions move to that character, so you can pick the conversation up again later.

What the Studio sees

The context view shows what is sent: The draft (every filled field) and, optionally, Reference cards drawn from a playlist of yours, as examples of what you like. Budget (tokens) caps how much of the reference cards is sent (2500 by default).

Studio settings

The gear (Studio settings) holds:

  • the connection the Studio uses and its overrides (see Connections);
  • Format as it streams: Same as Chat (the switch in Chat settings), On or Off, for whether an unfinished code block shows as a box while a reply arrives (other blocks and tables appear as soon as they are whole either way). It is greyed out while Stream the reply is off under Overrides. A reply shown as it is written follows to the bottom; scroll up and the view stays where you are, and scroll back to the bottom to follow it again;
  • Studio prompt: your own description of the Studio's role, in place of the built-in one (Load the Built-In Prompt to start from it, Back to Built-In to return). The notes on writing each field, the rule that keeps the draft's text quoted rather than obeyed, and the instructions for proposing changes are always added;
  • the saved prompts list;
  • Context budget (tokens): a ceiling on the whole request, 0 for none. Over it, the oldest messages of the conversation are left out; the draft and the reference cards are always sent whole.

Changes apply to the next message you send.

Media and galleries

Every character has a gallery: images, videos and audio kept with it on your server. Card text often links to images hosted elsewhere, and those links break when a host deletes them; downloading them into the gallery keeps the card whole.

The Gallery tab

A character's Gallery tab shows its images and videos in a grid, and audio files in their own section with players. Click a tile to open the viewer. Large galleries are split into pages.

To add files, drop them onto Drop files here to add to gallery, or click it to pick them: images, videos and audio are accepted. A file identical to one already in the gallery is not stored twice. A file whose name says the wrong kind (a video named like a picture, say) is stored and shown as what it really is.

Right-click a file (on a phone, press and hold it) for its menu: Open shows it in the viewer, Download saves it, and Delete removes it from the gallery for good, after asking.

The gallery shows images (PNG, JPEG, WebP, GIF, AVIF), video (MP4, WebM, MOV) and audio (MP3, OGG, WAV, M4A, FLAC).

The viewer

The full-screen viewer opens from a gallery tile, from View Gallery in a card's menu, or from the character's picture (it opens on the picture, with the gallery after it).

  1. Which item of how many.
  2. Open in new tab: the file on its own.
  3. The previous item (or ←, or a click on the image's left half).
  4. The next item (or →, or a click on the image's right half).
  5. The strip: jumps to any item. Videos play muted and looping, with their controls.

The mouse wheel zooms (up to 5 times), dragging moves a zoomed image, and clicking it or pressing 0 resets the zoom. Esc closes the viewer.

On a phone, swipe to step and double-tap to zoom.

A chat has its own gallery window beside the conversation; see Chat windows.

Downloading a card's remote media

Download Media on the Gallery tab (or in a card's right-click menu) downloads what the card links to into its gallery, in up to four passes:

  1. Embedded Media: images, audio and video linked from the card's text (description, first message, alternate greetings, scenario, creator's notes and the rest).
  2. Lorebook Media: the same from the embedded lorebook's entries, when Include Lorebook Media is on.
  3. The site's own gallery, for a linked character on a site that has one.
  4. External Galleries: albums and folders on image hosts that the card links to (below).

From the Gallery tab a window shows the download: a log of each file, so you can see why one failed. From a card's right-click menu it joins the background queue instead and you carry on. Either way, files you already have are skipped, by link and by content, so running it again only fetches what is new. Downloads go through your server; links to private or local network addresses are never fetched.

With Replace remote URLs with local files on (Settings > Media, on by default), the card's text shows your downloaded copies in place of the remote links: in the character's details and in chats. The card's text itself is not changed.

Downloading media for many characters

Select characters and press Media in the selection toolbar, or right-click one of them and pick Download Media. Each selected character with remote media or a site link joins the background queue, whatever the media setting says, and a message says how many were queued and how many had nothing to download. A selected character is scanned again even if its media was downloaded before, so new links are picked up.

Settings > Media > Bulk Operations > Download Media for All Characters queues every character in your library the same way. Characters already done are remembered and left out (Clear Processed History forgets that, so the next run goes over everything again). The notifications bell follows each download, and the queue carries on after a reload.

Media settings

Settings > Media, in its URL Replacement and Options groups:

SettingDefaultWhat it does
Replace remote URLs with local filesonShows downloaded copies in place of remote links
Include Provider GalleryonDownloads the site's own gallery for linked characters
Include Lorebook MediaoffAlso scans the embedded lorebook's entries
Include External GalleriesonFollows links to albums on image hosts
Fast skip (filename match)onSkips a site gallery file that is already there by name, without downloading it to compare
Import in the backgroundonOnline imports return at once and finish in a queue (see Browsing online)
When an import has extra mediaDownload in the BackgroundOr Ask Me (Show a Download Prompt), or Do Nothing

Civitai API Key (its Key) and Pixiv Login (your Pixiv PHPSESSID cookie) unlock content on those hosts, each with Test Connection; they are kept on your server. Save and Test Connection both hand over what you typed first: a key or cookie the server refuses (too long, or more than the session value) is said, and nothing is tested or saved.

External gallery hosts

When a card links to an album or folder on one of these hosts, the downloader opens it and fetches the images inside:

HostWhat it opensNeeds
Catboxalbumsnothing
Civitaiposts and imagesnothing for public posts; the API key for private or hidden ones
Dropboxshared folders and filesnothing
Google Driveshared foldersnothing
ImgBBalbums and image pagesnothing
Imgboxgalleriesnothing
Imgchestpostsnothing; a password-protected post works when the card's text holds the password
MEGAshared foldersnothing
Pixivartworksnothing; R-18 works need the Pixiv login, with R-18 viewing on in your Pixiv account (when the saved login cannot be used, an R-18 artwork's error says so: paste the PHPSESSID value again)
PostImggalleriesnothing

Background downloads

By default (When an import has extra media set to Download in the Background), the media of every online import is queued and downloaded one character at a time while you carry on. To be asked first instead, or to skip the media, change that setting under Settings > Media. The notifications bell follows the queue: the current download's progress (with Cancel this download), what is waiting, failures with Retry and Dismiss, and Clear finished. The queue survives a reload: unfinished downloads resume on the next visit, skipping files already fetched.

Thumbnails

The grid, the gallery and chat lists show small copies of images, made by the server the first time each is needed and kept on disk, so a large library scrolls smoothly; the viewer and the details always show the full image. There is nothing to set up.

A freshly imported or migrated library makes its thumbnails as you first browse it, which can feel slow once. Settings > Media > Bulk Operations > Generate Missing Thumbnails makes them all ahead of time.

Limits

A gallery upload or a downloaded file can be up to 200 MB; a download gives up after a minute. A card picture can be up to 10 MB when you replace it in the editor, and a persona picture up to 25 MB.

Chats and the conversation

Chats need a connection to an AI model; see Connections. Everything else on this page, such as reading, searching and exporting chats, works without one.

The Chats view

Chats in the view switcher lists every chat across your library, newest activity first by default, loading more as you scroll. Its controls in the top bar:

  1. The search box, which matches chat and character names. See Searching chats.
  2. Advanced Filters, with fields for chats.
  3. The sort: Most Recent, Oldest First, Character (A-Z), Character (Z-A), Most Messages, Least Messages, Most Chats (Character).
  4. The grouping: Recent, by time (one list) or By character (a collapsible group per character).
  5. The layout, for the single list: List (compact rows with date bands such as "Today" and "This week") or Cards (larger tiles with a preview).
  6. Refresh Chats.
  7. More Options.

The sort and grouping it opens with are Settings > General > View Defaults > Chats; the layout is remembered when you change it.

Each chat shows the character's picture, the chat's name, when it was last active, the character's name (click it to open the character), the message count, and the start of the last message. A chip shows when the chat is played as a different persona from your usual one, and Draft when you left an unsent message in it. With the list sorted by Most Recent and no search, the latest chat sits on top as a large Continue card.

Click a chat to open it. Right-click it (on a phone, long-press it or use its ⋮) for Open Chat, Lorebooks (bind lorebooks to this chat, see Lorebooks) and Delete Chat. On a computer, pointing at a chat also shows its lorebook and delete buttons.

Searching chats, including the words inside messages, is covered in Search and filters.

Starting a chat

  • Chat in a character's details resumes that character's latest chat, or starts the first one.
  • New Chat on a character's Chats tab always starts a fresh one.
  • Inside a conversation, New Chat in the options menu starts a fresh chat with the same character.

A new chat opens with the card's first message; the card's alternate greetings are the first message's alternates, so you can swipe through them before you reply.

The conversation

  1. Back to chats: closes the conversation (below).
  2. The character and the chat's name: click the name to open the character's details, the picture for its gallery.
  3. The chat windows: Personas, Assistant, Gallery, Background, Inspect the prompt, Lorebooks, Connections, Prompt manager, Regex scripts and Chat settings. Each is described in Chat windows. Extensions you switch on add their own buttons after these (see Extension windows).
  4. A message's actions, shown when you point at it: Edit message, Regenerate and More actions. See Messages.
  5. The last reply's alternates: Previous alternate, the counter and Next alternate. See Alternates.
  6. Chat options. See The options menu.
  7. Attach pictures. See Writing.
  8. Continue the last reply, direct the character's next reply, and Write my next message for me. See Writing.
  9. Send.

On a narrow window the window buttons that do not fit move behind More; on a phone they are tiles under Windows in the options menu. On a computer, a counter in the header shows the tokens used this session, as the model reports them.

Back to chats closes the conversation. Switching to another view instead parks it: a reply that is being written keeps going, the Chats tab shows a dot (pulsing while writing, solid when a new reply has landed), and coming back returns you to where you were.

On a phone

On a phone the chat fills the screen, so parking it has a button of its own:

  1. Keep the chat running and browse the library: minimizes the chat, like a window. A reply that is being written keeps going in the background while you browse your characters, the Chats tab shows a dot (pulsing while it writes, solid once the reply has landed), and tapping Chats brings you straight back to the conversation, right where you left it. The back arrow, by contrast, closes the chat.
  2. Chat options: everything else, the chat windows included as tiles under Windows.

Writing

Type in Write a message... and press Send, or Enter on a computer (Shift+Enter starts a new line). On a phone, Enter always starts a new line and you send with the button. Sending an empty box asks the character for another reply.

The buttons beside the box:

  • Continue the last reply: the character carries on from where its last message ended.
  • Direct the character's next reply: what you typed steers the next reply instead of being sent as your message. Swiping or regenerating that reply follows the same direction.
  • Write my next message for me: the model drafts your next message into the box; nothing is sent until you send it. Anything already typed steers the draft.
  • Attach pictures (the paperclip): pictures for your next message. You can also paste a picture into the box or drop one onto the chat. Each lands in the chat's own gallery and waits above the box until you send; its × takes it off this message and leaves it in the gallery. A message can be pictures alone, with no text. The paperclip shows only when the chat's connection is set as seeing pictures (Sees pictures set to Yes, see Connections); set to No or Not checked, it is gone.

A message's pictures show under its text; click one to see it full screen. The model sees every picture in the chat that fits the context budget, the character's included: a picture on one of the character's messages goes to the model just before that reply.

Your unsent text is saved as you type, so it is still there when you come back, even on another device.

Macros in your message (such as {{char}}) are filled in when you send it. See Macros.

The options menu

The ☰ button (Chat options) beside the box holds:

  • Display: Background, Show Every Message Action (shows each message's actions without pointing at it), and Open Thinking by Default.
  • This chat: Chat Card (this chat's own scenario, examples, main prompt and Author's note, see The chat's own card), Rename Chat, New Chat, Copy as Markdown, Export JSONL, Character Details and Delete Chat.
  • Messages: Regenerate Last Reply, Continue Last Reply, Direct the Character's Next Reply, Write my next message, Copy Last Reply, and the Keep a Stopped Reply switch.
  • Extensions: actions that extensions add, when there are any.

Messages

Each message shows its author's picture and name, its time, and its actions. Turn on Reply cost in Chat settings to see each message's number and token count too.

The actions (pointing at a message on a computer; the ⋯ on a phone, which opens a Message sheet):

  • Edit message: edit the text in place, then Save or Cancel.
  • Regenerate: on the last character message, writes it again.
  • Under More actions:
    • Copy source: copies the raw text.
    • Branch from here: starts a new chat holding everything up to this message, named after this chat with "Branch #N", keeping its lorebooks, persona and background, and opens it. The chat's own card texts and Author's note come along, and with Memory or Trackers on, their state as of this message.
    • Continue this reply: on the last character message.
    • Hide from the prompt: the message stays on screen, dimmed and marked hidden, but the model does not see it. Send to the model again undoes it.
    • Delete message: for a message with alternates, you choose the whole message or just the alternate shown.

A divider marked Context starts here appears above the oldest message that still fits the context budget; older messages are kept but not sent. See The context budget.

Alternates

The last character message carries its alternates, with Previous alternate, a counter such as "2/5", and Next alternate. On the last alternate the right arrow becomes Write another reply and generates a new one, keeping the others. With ← and → you can do the same from the keyboard. An alternate opens at its first line; when it runs past the bottom of the screen, Jump to latest takes you to its end.

Regenerate rewrites the alternate you are looking at instead of adding one; use Write another reply when you want to keep the current one. The first message's alternates are the card's alternate greetings: they cycle round and never generate.

Once you reply, earlier messages keep their alternates but show only the one you chose.

Combine alternates into a new one (shown with two or more alternates) opens the swipe combiner: tick the alternates to merge, optionally guide the mix, and add the result as a new alternate. See Combining alternates.

While a reply is written

The reply streams in as it is written. Scroll up, however little, and the view stays where you are; Jump to latest appears once there is more below to return to. Scroll back to the bottom and let go, and it follows the reply again. While it follows, the bottom stays in view when the phone's keyboard opens or the box you type in grows. When the app is in a background tab, the tab's title flashes when a reply lands (see Flash the tab when a reply lands in Chat settings).

Stop ends the reply. With Keep a Stopped Reply off (the default) the half-written reply is dropped and the chat is as it was; with it on, what arrived is kept, marked "interrupted", so you can edit or continue it. Leaving the chat, reloading or closing the tab while a reply is written counts as stopping it.

Models that think before answering show their thinking in a Thinking fold above the reply, closed unless Open Thinking by Default is on. A closed fold is filled the first time you open it, so a long chat opens quickly; your browser's find doesn't reach inside a fold you haven't opened unless Render closed thinking up front is on in Chat settings.

When a reply fails, the reason shows above the box: no connection set up, a model that spent its whole reply budget thinking, an empty reply, a timeout. The server retries a couple of times on its own before giving up when the failure looks temporary; after that, send again or regenerate.

Formatting

Messages render markdown: paragraphs, headings, lists, quotes, tables, bold, italics (shown as narration), strikethrough, inline code and code blocks (with Copy code). Text in quotation marks is coloured as dialogue. Images and links in a message show, links opening in a new tab. Complete HTML blocks render when Render HTML blocks is on, with scripts removed. Styles that come with them (a preset's regex scripts often draw styled cards) apply to their own message only, never to the rest of the app or to other messages. A block wider than the message (a wide table, a card's own layout) scrolls sideways inside itself instead of stretching the message. See Chat settings for the options.

Renaming, exporting and deleting a chat

From the options menu:

  • Rename Chat: chat names are unique per character.
  • Export JSONL: downloads the chat as a JSONL file in SillyTavern's chat format.
  • Copy as Markdown: copies the whole chat as text, each message under its speaker's name.
  • Delete Chat: removes the chat and every message in it; the character is untouched.

Chats cannot be imported one at a time; see Importing and exporting.

Several tabs and devices

Every open tab follows changes made elsewhere on the same account: a reply written in another tab appears when it finishes, a rename updates the header, and a chat deleted elsewhere closes with a note. If you try to edit, delete or regenerate a message that changed in another tab since you last saw it, nothing is written and the chat reloads, so an older screen never overwrites newer text.

Group chats

Every chat has one character; group chats are not supported.

Keys

In an open chat, when you are not typing in a box: / jumps to the message box, End to the latest message, ← and → page the last reply's alternates, R regenerates, C continues, I writes your next message, and E edits the last message. Esc closes an open menu or the top chat window, then the conversation (stopping a reply that is being written); in the message box it only leaves the box. Shift+Esc stops a reply from anywhere. The rest are in Keyboard shortcuts and gestures.

Chat windows

The buttons in a conversation's header open windows beside the chat: settings, the prompt, regex scripts, the lorebooks, the persona, the background, the gallery and the assistant. This page covers each. The conversation itself is in Chats and the conversation.

How the windows work

On a computer each window floats over the conversation. Drag its title to move it, drag an edge or corner to resize it, and double-click an edge to put it back. Clicking a window brings it to the front, and Esc closes the front one. Windows remember their size and place; Settings > General > Theme & Layout > Reset Window Layouts puts them all back. With One panel at a time on (in Chat settings, below), opening a window closes the others.

Tip

The conversation is a window too: on a computer, drag its header to move it and its edges to resize it. Double-click a window's title bar, or the conversation's header, to put it back where it started.

Arrange the header

On a computer the header's buttons are yours to arrange, the ones extensions add included:

  • Reorder: drag a button left or right; a bar shows where it lands, and Esc cancels the drag.
  • Group: drop a button on a group to file it there (rest on the group and it opens, so you can drop it at a spot in its list). A group shows as one icon with a small corner mark; click it for its buttons.
  • Right-click a button, a group, a divider or the header itself for the rest: Move Left and Move Right, Move to Group (or New Group, which asks you to pick its icon), Add Divider, Hide, and on a group Rename, Change Icon and Ungroup.
  • To bring a hidden button back, right-click the header, choose Hidden Buttons and pick it; it returns to its place. Reset Header puts everything back as it ships.

From the keyboard, the menu key opens the same menu on the button you are on, and the arrow keys move through it. Chat settings > Conversation header lists every button with a switch to show or hide it.

On a phone every window opens full screen, with a back arrow; the phone's back button closes it. The windows are tiles under Windows in the conversation's options menu.

Chat settings

Chat settings holds the settings that apply to every chat. Find a setting searches them.

GroupSettingDefaultWhat it does
ReadingLine lengthFillHow long a line may get before it wraps; far right removes the limit
Message fillonDraws each message on its own plate
Message layoutAuto (Full Width on Mobile)Text beside the avatar, or full width under it
DisplayLayoutBannerBanner puts each message on a card; Column sets the text in a bubble under the name
