On a computer, a Conversation header group lists every button in the header, in its order, with a switch to show or hide it, its groups and dividers, and Reset Header (see Arrange the header). When extensions add chat windows, an Extensions group lists them with a switch for the phone's tiles.

The prompt manager

Prompt manager controls what is sent to the model and in what order. It works on presets: a preset is an ordered list of blocks. With no preset chosen, Built-In Defaults apply: the main prompt, the character's description, personality and scenario, lore before and after the character, your persona's description, the example dialogue, the chat history, and the post-history instructions. A preset whose blocks all send nothing for a reply is refused with a message saying so, and nothing in the chat changes.

  1. The preset this chat uses, below.
  2. How many tokens the preset's own written blocks add to every prompt.
  3. Preset actions, below.
  4. Whose choice the preset is, with Make Default, Use Default or Clear Default.
  5. Filter blocks and contents.
  6. Drag a block to a new place; the icon beside it says whether it is your own text or a marker the app fills.
  7. Edit this block, Duplicate this block and Delete this block.
  8. The switch turns the block on or off.
  9. Text Block, Marker, Group and Sub-group: add a block.

The menu beside the preset's name (3) has Preset Settings, New Preset, Duplicate Preset, Rename Preset, Import a Preset File, Export This Preset, Export as a SillyTavern Preset and Delete Preset.

The preset you pick is for the open chat alone: picking one never changes any other chat. The first choice, Same as the Default (with the default's name), puts the chat back on the default. The line under the preset's name says whose choice it is, and only its buttons change the default:

  • This chat's own: the chat keeps the preset you picked, and the Prompts button wears a dot. Make Default makes it the default as well, for every chat without one of its own. Use Default puts the chat back on the default.
  • The default: the chat follows the default. Clear Default puts every chat following it on the built-in blocks.

The blocks shown are the ones this chat sends, so an edit changes what this chat sends (and every chat using the same preset). A preset you make, duplicate or import becomes this chat's own. Built-In Defaults can be the default but not a chat's own; to give one chat the built-in blocks, make a new preset (it starts from them). Deleting a chat's own preset puts the chat back on the default; deleting the default puts every chat following it on the built-in blocks. A branch keeps its chat's preset.

Each block can be switched on or off, dragged to a new place (on a phone, moved up and down from its menu), edited, duplicated and deleted. At the bottom: Text Block (your own text), Marker (a slot the app fills, such as the chat history or lore), Group and Sub-group (to organize long presets). A block's editor has:

  • Name.
  • Sent as: system, user or assistant.
  • Placement: Before the Chat History, After the Chat History, or Inside the Chat History, at a Depth (with a depth and an order).
  • Fires on: Normal reply, Continue, Impersonate, Swipe and Regenerate. With none ticked the block is sent on every reply; tick some and it is sent only on those, and its row wears a small filter mark. A SillyTavern preset's own choices come across when you import it; a choice this app never makes (SillyTavern's Quiet) shows as a tick of its own, so you can see it and untick it.
  • Ignore the character's own version of this block, for the main prompt and the post-history instructions: by default a card's own system prompt and post-history instructions replace the preset's, and {{original}} inside the card's text stands for the preset's.
  • The content, where macros work (see Macros).

If the chat history block is switched off, the window warns you: every reply would be written without the conversation.

The Character's picture marker shows the model the character's avatar, so a reply can draw on how they look. It is not in the built-in blocks: Marker adds the markers a preset lacks, one at a time, and a new block starts switched off. It always goes in your turn, before or after the chat history, and is left out when the connection does not see pictures.

Import a Preset File reads this app's own preset files and SillyTavern chat-completion presets. A setting this version does not know, in a file from another version of this app, is left out and named when you import. From a SillyTavern preset it takes the prompts and their order; samplers and connection settings are left out (set those on the connection), and a report lists what did not come across. A preset that carries regex scripts brings them along, off: the report says how many and has a switch to allow them (see Regex scripts). A file of this app's whose regex script is not in a shape it reads is refused, nothing is made, and the message names the script by its place in the file.

Every preset builds its prompt the way SillyTavern does, so a SillyTavern preset behaves here as its author tested it: each block goes as its own message, the card's example dialogue goes as separate turns, and your persona goes as you wrote it. One thing differs on purpose: an example with no line starting with a speaker's name and a colon (the names written another way, or plain prose) is sent whole, as one piece of text, where SillyTavern sends none of it. An imported preset brings its own wording for the settings below; a preset you make starts from this app's. Export as a SillyTavern Preset writes the preset as a SillyTavern file, its regex scripts included; anything SillyTavern has no place for (the character's picture block, the directed-reply wording) is named when you export.

Preset Settings opens the preset's settings in place of the list (the built-in defaults show theirs, read only). Each changed field has Back to Default, and nothing is kept until Save; closing with changes not saved asks first:

  • Wrapping: the text sent around the character's Personality, the Scenario and the Lore placed before or after the character. Each part goes where its marker is ({{personality}}, {{scenario}}, {{lore}}); an empty box sends the part as it is.
  • Messages: a Conversation opener sent just before the oldest message, a line sent Before each example, and Join neighbouring system blocks, which sends system blocks that sit together as one message.
  • Requests: your own wording for Write my next message, Direct the character's next reply and Continue last reply (an empty box sends nothing), what to Join the new words with when a reply is continued, and End on the reply itself, which ends the prompt on the reply so far instead of sending the continue wording. Many presets tell the model never to write as you, and it then refuses Write my next message however it is asked: rewording it here is the fix.

A box holds up to 20,000 characters. If one is longer, nothing in that Save is kept and a message says why; Preset Settings opens again on what you typed, for you to shorten. Your other changes to the preset still save.

Regex scripts

A regex script finds a pattern in the text and replaces it: hiding a status block on screen, tidying a model's habits out of what it reads, swapping a name. They work as SillyTavern's do, and a script file from SillyTavern imports as it is. The Regex scripts button (beside the prompt manager's) opens the window; on a phone it is the Regex tile.

Scripts come from three places, run in this order, each its own tab:

  • Yours: run in every chat.
  • Preset: the scripts the chat's preset carries.
  • Character: the scripts on the character's card.

A preset's and a character's scripts stay off until you allow them. While a preset or a card with scripts waits for an answer, the button and its tab wear an orange dot (in a narrow window, the More button the Regex button waits behind wears it), and the tab offers Allow or Leave Them Off. After that a Run these scripts switch on the tab changes your mind. Saving a new script into a list that is not allowed does not allow it. Your answer stands for the scripts you saw: replace a character with a card that carries different scripts and they wait for a new answer, dot and all.

Each script has its own switch, and a tap opens it. Runs on says which text it reads: your messages, AI replies, narrator lines, lorebook entries or reasoning. What it changes decides where the result goes:

  • What you see: only on screen.
  • What the AI reads: only in what is sent.
  • Both: on screen and in what is sent.
  • The message itself: as if the message had been written that way, on screen and in what is sent, while the stored message keeps its words. Switch on Change what is saved to rewrite messages as they are stored instead, as SillyTavern does: when you send one, when a reply arrives, on a new chat's greeting, and on an edit when Run when a message is edited is on.

Advanced holds the rest: Min depth and Max depth set how far back a message may be for the script to run on it (0 is the newest, empty is any; Min depth skips the messages newer than it, Max depth the ones older), Macros in Find fills macros like {{char}} into the pattern, Trim out cuts text out of what was found before it goes back, and Run when a message is edited decides whether a message you edited by hand still takes the script. Try it runs the script on any text you type and shows the result on screen and as the AI reads it (for a script that changes what is saved, what the save leaves).

Editing a message by hand opens it as its scripts wrote it, so a line a script cut stays cut once you save the edit. Continue picks up from the reply as its scripts wrote it, so the new part never vanishes behind a line a script cuts. Hidden messages still show through their scripts, and a reply that a script changes on screen is drawn formatted as it streams, even with Format as it streams off, so its blocks never show as code.

A pattern that never finishes is stopped after a quarter of a second and wears Too slow until you save it again, and the scripts after it carry on; one that does not compile wears Broken pattern. Reorder scripts by dragging their handle, or with Move Up and Move Down in a script's menu. Import and Export read and write SillyTavern's script files.

The context budget

Every prompt has to fit the model's context. The budget is the connection's Context size when it sets one, otherwise Context budget in Chat settings (8192 tokens), minus Reserve for the reply. When a chat outgrows it, the oldest messages are left out first; the newest two are always sent, and the card, lore and examples are never cut. A Context starts here divider in the chat marks the oldest message still sent.

Lorebook entries have a budget of their own inside it: Budget (% of context) in the scan settings (see Scan settings). Hidden messages are never sent.

A picture costs as much as a page or more of text. When a chat with pictures outgrows the budget, the oldest pictures are sent as a line of text ([picture: its name]) before any message is left out, and the newest message's pictures are always sent as pictures. A connection's Most pictures a request does the same past its limit.

The prompt inspector

Inspect the prompt builds the exact prompt the next reply would get and shows it message by message: how many messages and tokens, how many old turns were left out to fit, and how many pictures go. Each message shows the pictures it carries above its text, and a note at the top says when older pictures go as text to fit, or when the connection is not known to see pictures and none are sent. A macro that nothing fills is flagged, since it would be sent as written.

  1. Copy the whole prompt.
  2. Rebuild: builds it again.
  3. Find in the prompt: Enter goes to the next match, and arrows for the previous and next match appear beside it.

Tip

The inspector is a dry run of a plain reply: a lorebook entry with a trigger chance is shown as if its roll passed, and what you have typed in the message box is not in it yet.

The chat's own card

Chat Card in the options menu (the window is This chat's card) holds texts that belong to this chat alone.

Card Texts overrides the card's Scenario, Example dialogue and Main prompt for this chat only, leaving the character untouched. An empty field uses the card's own. Remove All clears them.

Author's Note is a note sent with the chat, for steering the story:

  • the note itself;
  • Placement: Before the main prompt, After the main prompt, or In the chat, at a depth (how many messages back from the newest, set under Depth);
  • Voice: whether it is sent as system, user or assistant;
  • Frequency: every so many of your messages (1 is every reply, 0 never).

Default for new chats sets the note new chats start with. Under Lorebook scanning, Scan the note lets lorebook entries fire on the note's words (this applies to every chat).

Background

Background sets a wallpaper behind the conversation. Pick where it applies: This Chat, This Character or Everywhere; the most specific one wins.

  1. Where it applies: This Chat, This Character or Everywhere.
  2. Where images come from: Library, Gallery or Add.
  3. An image's rename and delete buttons, shown when you point at it (in the Library).
  4. How the image is drawn.
  5. No Background.
  6. Inherit.
  7. Apply.

Images come from three tabs: Library (images you have used before, which you can rename or delete), Gallery (the character's picture and gallery), and Add (a file from your device, or an image link with Fetch). Then adjust Fit (Cover, Contain, Stretch, Center or Tile), Opacity, Blur, Brightness, Saturate and Resolution. Changes preview live; Apply keeps them, and closing without it puts the old wallpaper back. No Background shows none here, Inherit goes back to the wider setting.

Gallery

Gallery shows the character's picture and gallery beside the chat, one large image with a strip below, and the chat's own gallery after them: pictures that belong to this chat alone, like the ones you attach to your messages. A chat's picture wears a small speech-bubble mark in the corner of its thumbnail.

Drop images onto the window, or press Add pictures, to add them. They go to the character's gallery unless you choose otherwise in Gallery options (the sliders button), which also holds the Show ticks: The character's pictures and This chat's pictures, each on or off, and Add new pictures to: The character's gallery or This chat's gallery. A picture you add to a gallery you have hidden is added all the same, and the message after the upload says so.

  1. Add pictures.
  2. Creator's notes.
  3. Set as background: opens Background with the picture, a chat's picture included.
  4. Attach to your next message: puts the picture beside the message box, ready to send; a character's picture is copied into this chat's gallery first. It shows only when the chat's connection is set as seeing pictures.
  5. Grid view, where Search by filename joins the row.
  6. Full screen.
  7. Gallery options.
  8. The arrows step to the previous and next picture.
  9. The strip: every picture; click one to show it.

On a chat's own picture, Remove from this chat's gallery joins the row: any message that showed it stops showing it, and the confirmation says how many do. Keys: ← → step, G grid, / search, F full screen, B set as background, A attach to your next message, N creator's notes, + and - zoom, 0 reset. With a mouse, the wheel zooms the picture, dragging moves a zoomed picture, and a click puts it back. On a phone, swipe to step and tap an image to zoom it.

A chat's gallery goes with the chat: a branch takes a copy of it, deleting the chat deletes it, and an export that includes media carries it, each message showing the same pictures when the pack is imported again.

Combining alternates

Combine alternates into a new one, on a message with two or more alternates, opens the swipe combiner. Tick the alternates to draw from, optionally write how to mix them in Guide the mix (optional), and generate. Then keep the result with Add as Swipe, try again with Retry, or go Back. It uses the chat's connection, and sends only the alternates you ticked, the names and your guide text, not the rest of the chat. Closing the combiner while it works (with Esc, going back, or switching chats) or opening it on another message stops the generation, so a mix only ever lands on the message it was made from.

  1. An alternate: its box puts it in the mix or leaves it out, and its name shows it below.
  2. The alternate you are looking at.
  3. Guide the mix (optional).
  4. Generates the combined alternate (or press Enter).

Tip

It works on the opening message too, while that is still the last message: a way to merge a card's alternate greetings into a greeting of your own.

The Assistant

Assistant ( in the conversation's header) opens an out-of-character writing partner for the story. It reads the character card, the lorebooks in force, and the recent messages, and talks with you about the story: what a scene is doing, where it could go, whether someone is acting out of character. It never writes the roleplay itself.

  1. Session: this chat's Assistant conversations; pick one to switch to it.
  2. New session, Rename this session and Delete this session.
  3. What it can see: the card, the sources, the messages and the lorebooks it is sent, and the switches for proposed edits. See What it sees.
  4. Assistant settings: its connection, its prompt and how much it reads. See Assistant settings.
  5. Saved prompts: questions you keep; picking one fills the box, it does not send.
  6. Attach pictures, below.
  7. Send, which turns into Stop while an answer arrives.

Type in Ask about the story..., or start from one of the suggestions on an empty session. Each message can be copied, edited, asked again from that point, or deleted. Closing the window does not stop an answer in progress; switching chats does.

The paperclip beside Send shows the Assistant pictures: Pictures from this device (they go into this chat's gallery, see Gallery), the character's portrait, or the character's gallery, which opens the character's gallery pictures over the box: tap a picture to add it, tap it again to take it off, and Done closes them. You can also paste a picture into the box or drop one on the window. They wait above the box until you send, and a message can be pictures alone. The paperclip shows only when the Assistant's connection is set as seeing pictures; a picture it cannot see is described to it by name.

Sessions

Assistant conversations are saved per chat. The session menu (1 above) switches between them; New session, Rename this session and Delete this session (2) manage them. Deleting a session leaves your chat and lorebooks untouched.

What it sees

What it can see (the at the right end of the session row, 3 above) swaps the conversation for exactly what is sent; press it again to go back.

In short:

  • the whole character card, creator's notes and Character's Note included (the creator's notes the roleplay model never sees), and this chat's own card texts;
  • the Sources you leave on (below);
  • the most recent messages, up to Messages it can see (20 by default); hidden messages are left out;
  • the lorebooks this chat can reach: entries that fired, in full, and the rest by title only.

Sources lists what else the assistant can be given, each with a switch and its size:

  • Persona (on): who you play here, with the persona's name, pronouns and description, so it knows which character is yours.
  • Author's Note (on): the note this chat sends, where it goes and how often, and whether it is this chat's own or the default.
  • Chat facts (on): the chat's name, how many messages it holds, and the chat it branches from.
  • Preset text blocks (off): the text blocks your preset sends, as written. A long preset makes every question cost more.

An extension can add sources of its own; they show in the same list with their own switches. A source you switch off is not read at all. Whatever it is given, the assistant never decides what your character does, says or feels in anything it drafts: it leaves your part to you, or asks.

It can ask to read more (another alternate of a message, or a lorebook entry in full), at most once per answer, and you see a note when it does.

Proposed edits

When you allow it, the assistant can propose edits instead of only describing them:

  • Let it propose lorebook edits: new entries, changed entries, deletions.
  • Let it propose message edits: rewriting a message (the alternate on screen), replacing a passage, hiding a message, deleting one.
  • Let it propose card edits: the character card's fields (the Character's Note among them), its tags and its alternate greetings. Every chat with the character reads the card, and a greeting opens new chats, not this one. The card is saved to its Version History before anything is written, so an applied change can be undone from there. These snapshots keep a limit of their own (Max Chat Assistant snapshots per character, under Version History in Settings), so a long session never pushes out the card's other automatic snapshots.
  • Let it propose edits to this chat's notes: the Author's Note, and this chat's own scenario, example dialogue and main prompt. Only this chat changes: a chat using the default note gets a note of its own, and the card stays as it is. The Author's Note can only be changed while its source is on.
  • Let it propose persona edits: your persona's description. A persona is shared, so the change reaches every chat that uses it. It can only be changed while the Persona source is on.

All of them are off by default. With the notes or persona switch on and its source switched off, a note under the switch says what it cannot change until the source is back on. A card or persona change says on its card what it reaches beyond this chat. Proposals arrive under Proposed changes, each with a box to include it, a side-by-side comparison, a touch-up editor, Apply this change now (Write this to the card now on a card change), and a way to set it aside. Apply Selected writes the ticked ones after a confirmation. Every change is checked again just before it is written, so one aimed at something that has since changed or vanished is refused rather than misapplied. A refused change (marked in yellow or red, with the reason under it) offers Try again : the assistant is told what went wrong, re-reads the story as it is now and proposes the change again, or says it does not fit any more. A reply with several refused changes also offers Try All Again in its footer. Try again shows only while that kind of edit is switched on, since the assistant can propose it only then. A new lorebook entry goes into a book you pick, or a new book linked to the character. An applied change is written at once and has no undo (a card change aside, through its Version History), so read the comparison first.

The assistant only ever builds on what you applied: once it has replied again, it is not shown the changes of an earlier reply you left unapplied, so it never treats one as part of the story.

Assistant settings

The gear (Assistant settings, 4 above) holds, for every chat:

  • Answers with: the connection the assistant uses, which can differ from the chat's (a cheaper model is often enough), with Reasoning effort, Max reply tokens and Temperature.
  • Assistant prompt: your own description of the assistant's role, in place of the built-in one. The rules that keep story text quoted rather than obeyed, and the edit instructions, are always added.
  • The saved prompts list.
  • Messages it can see and Context budget (tokens) (0 for no limit).
  • Show the reply as it is written (off by default). While a reply is written it follows to the bottom; scroll up and the view stays where you are, and scroll back to the bottom to follow it again, as in the chat.
  • Format as it streams: Same as Chat (the switch in Chat settings), On or Off, for whether an unfinished code block shows as a box while a reply arrives (other blocks and tables appear as soon as they are whole either way). It is greyed out while Show the reply as it is written is off, since only a reply shown as it arrives has anything to format early.
  • The proposed-edit switches above.

Personas, Lorebooks and Connections

These windows have their own pages: Personas, Lorebooks and Connections.

Extension windows

Extensions can add windows of their own (Memory, Trackers, Story Beats, Translator, Chat Timeline, among the bundled ones). They open from the header like the built-in ones, and their buttons arrange like the others (Arrange the header); Chat settings > Extensions chooses whether each has a phone tile. See Extensions.

Connections

A connection is an AI endpoint the app can send a prompt to: a hosted service or a model server you run yourself. Chats, the Assistant, the Studio, the Card Recommender, AI lorebook generation, the CSS Assistant and several extensions need one. The library, search, editing and online browsing do not.

The app calls them profiles in Settings and connections in a chat; they are the same thing.

Adding a connection

Settings > AI > Profiles lists your connections. Press Add Profile and the editor opens below the list on a new one called "New profile"; name it there, fill in the endpoint, key and model, and press Save. In a chat, Connections in the header does the same with New Connection.

Until the connection a chat uses can send, the chat says so above the message box and names what is missing (an endpoint, a model, or a kind whose extension is switched off); Finish Setting It Up opens it in Connections, and the note goes once it is ready.

Each row shows whether the connection is ready (an endpoint, a key and a model are set) and which kind and model it uses; Edit opens it again.

The connection editor

The same editor opens from Settings and from a chat.

Connection:

  • Name: what you call it.
  • Kind: how the request is sent (see Kinds).
  • Endpoint: the address of the API, for example https://api.example.com/v1.
  • API key: Add Key to enter one, then Replace or Remove later. The key is stored on the server and never sent back to the browser; the editor only ever shows that a key is stored and its last four characters. Replacing or removing it takes effect when you save.
  • Model: type a model name, or press the refresh button (List the models this endpoint offers) to pick from the endpoint's list, which also shows each model's context length when the endpoint reports it, and an eye beside a model the endpoint says can see pictures.
  • Context size: the prompt budget for this connection in tokens. Picking a model from the list fills it in when the endpoint reports one. Blank uses the app-wide Context budget from Chat settings (see The context budget).
  • Sees pictures: whether pictures you attach reach the model: Yes, No, or Not checked. Picking a model from the list fills it in when the endpoint says, unless the field already holds an answer (yours, or one saved before). Check shows the model two small pictures and asks it to name their colours: if it names both, it sees; if it answers without them, or the endpoint refuses pictures, it does not; and if the check could not be made (a wrong key, no answer) the field stays as it was and the note under it says why. Save keeps the answer. The paperclip that attaches pictures shows only for Yes. Only a connection set to Yes is sent pictures: one set to No or Not checked gets none, not even the pictures already in a chat. Some kinds of connection cannot carry pictures at all, and the field says so.
  • Most pictures a request: for an endpoint that takes only so many pictures in one request. Past it, a chat sends its oldest pictures as text, the newest message's always as pictures. Blank is no limit.

Sampling: Temperature, Top P, Top K, Max reply tokens, Presence penalty, Frequency penalty, Seed and Reasoning effort (low, medium or high, for models that reason). A blank field sends nothing, so the provider's own default applies, which is not the same as sending zero.

Test sends a tiny request through the connection as it stands in the editor, before you save, and says whether it answered. Save keeps your changes, Cancel drops them. The menu by the name (Connection actions) has Duplicate (key included) and Delete; chats that used a deleted connection fall back to the first one left.

Kinds

  • OpenAI-compatible (built in): any service or local model server that offers the OpenAI-style chat completions API. The endpoint is required and must include the API's version path (it usually ends in /v1); the app adds the rest. The key is optional, for servers that need none.
  • Anthropic Messages API: Anthropic's own API, added by the bundled Anthropic extension. Switch it on under Settings > Extensions first (see Extensions). The endpoint is optional, the key is your Anthropic API key, and settings that newer models refuse are left out for you.

A model server on your own network works as a connection: the app's block on private network addresses applies to downloads and online sites, not to your connections.

Choosing the connection for chats

In a chat, Connections lists your connections, and the line at the top says which one answers this chat and why.

  1. The connection that answers this chat, and why.
  2. New Connection.
  3. Answer this chat only with this connection.
  4. Make this the default for every chat.
  5. Edit.

The two buttons on each row:

  • The globe (Make this the default for every chat) makes it the default: every chat without a connection of its own uses it. The default's row says default.
  • The speech bubble (Answer this chat only with this connection) gives the open chat that connection alone, whatever the default becomes. Its row says this chat, and the Connections button wears a dot while the chat has its own.

Use Default at the top puts the chat back on the default. A connection you make with New Connection is not in use until you press its globe or its speech bubble. With no default chosen, or after it is deleted, chats use the first connection you created, and a chat whose own connection is deleted goes back to the default. A branch keeps its chat's connection.

Which connection each feature uses

FeatureWhere you choose
Chat replies and combining alternatesThe chat's Connections window: the default for every chat, or one for this chat only
The AssistantAssistant settings > Answers with (The Assistant)
The StudioStudio settings (Creating characters)
The Card RecommenderIts connection menu at the top (The library)
The CSS AssistantIts Connection menu (Settings and themes)
Generating lorebook entriesThe Connection step of the generator (Lorebooks)
Extensions that call a modelSettings > Extensions, the extension's own Connection setting; by default The one the chat uses for an extension that works on a chat, The one your chats use for one that does not

Overrides

Where a tool has its own connection menu (the Studio, the Card Recommender, the CSS Assistant, lorebook generation), an Overrides button beside it changes the request without touching the saved connection: Temperature, Reasoning and Max reply tokens, plus Stream the reply for tools that show the reply as it is written. Blank fields use the connection's own settings.

Timeouts and retries

A reply that sends nothing for five minutes before its first word, or stalls for two minutes in the middle, is given up on. Before the first word arrives, the server retries a temporary failure up to twice on its own.

Lorebooks

A lorebook (also called world info) is a set of entries, each a piece of background the model should know only when it matters: a place, a person, a rule of the setting. An entry has keywords; when one of them comes up in the recent messages, the entry's text is added to the prompt. Constant entries are always sent.

Where a lorebook applies

A book reaches a chat in any of four ways, and a chat uses every book that reaches it:

  • Linked to the character: in every chat with that character (see Lorebooks on a character).
  • Bound to one chat: in that chat only (see Lorebooks in one chat).
  • Bound to a persona: in every chat where you play that persona (see Personas).
  • Global: in every chat.

A card can also carry a book inside it, the embedded lorebook. An embedded book is never used in chats until you bring it into your library (see The embedded lorebook).

The Lorebooks manager

More Options > Lorebooks opens the manager: your books on the left, the selected book on the right. Each book shows its entry count and where it is used (characters, chats, global, personas).

  1. New: an empty book. See Creating, importing and exporting.
  2. The other ways to create one: Generate with AI.
  3. Import.
  4. Search lorebooks, characters, chats...: finds books by name, and also the books bound to a character, chat or persona whose name starts with what you type.
  5. The chips filter the list: All, Linked to characters, Bound to chats, Active in every chat, Bound to personas, Not bound anywhere; the ghost shows placeholder books, names something is bound to that have no book yet.
  6. The sort: Name A-Z, Name Z-A, Most Entries, Most Bound, Created (Newest), Created (Oldest), Modified (Newest), Modified (Oldest).
  7. The book's Rename, Duplicate, Export JSON and Delete.
  8. Where the book is used. See Binding a book.
  9. Search entries....
  10. opens or closes every entry, AI generates entries, and Add Entry adds one.
  11. Save: edits to the entries wait for it.
  12. An entry's switch: on or off.
  13. An entry's duplicate and delete.

↑ and ↓ step through the books. On a phone the list and the book take turns on screen.

Edits to a book's entries are held until you press Save; switching books or closing with unsaved edits asks first. If the book changed somewhere else meanwhile, or the check cannot tell, saving asks whether to overwrite it.

Creating, importing and exporting

  • New creates an empty book (type its name and press Enter). The arrow beside it offers Generate with AI (see Generating entries with AI).
  • Import reads .json files: SillyTavern world files, a card's lorebook on its own, or a whole card's JSON (its lorebook is taken out). Several files at once are fine. A name you already use gets a number, such as "Name (2)".
  • On a book: Rename (everything bound to it follows the new name), Duplicate, Export JSON (downloads it in the SillyTavern world format, including unsaved edits) and Delete (its bindings go with it).
  • Add a Note keeps a note about the book inside it, so it travels with exports.

Binding a book

The top of an open book shows where it is used, in four groups:

  • Characters: click + (Link to characters) to pick characters, with a search, a playlist filter and Hide already-linked; the × on a character unlinks it.
  • Chats: + (Bind to chats) picks chats the same way.
  • Personas: + (Bind to a persona) picks a persona.
  • Global: the switch makes the book Active everywhere.
  1. Search characters....
  2. Narrows the list to one playlist.
  3. Ticks every character shown, or clears them.
  4. Hide already-linked.
  5. A character: click its row to tick it; clicking its picture opens the character instead.
  6. Link: links the book to the ticked characters.

Entries

Each entry is a row with its switch, title, keywords, position and order; click it to open it. Search entries... filters them, the sort orders them (By Order, By Title, Oldest First, Newest First), and Add Entry adds one. Each entry can be duplicated or deleted.

The fields:

  • Title / Memo: a label for you.
  • Status: Normal (sent when a keyword matches), Constant (always sent) or Vectorized.
  • Content: the text added to the prompt.

Activation

  • Primary Keywords: type a keyword and press Enter or a comma. A keyword written as /pattern/flags is a regular expression.
  • Optional Filter: secondary keywords, with Logic deciding how they combine with the primary ones: AND ANY (one must also match), AND ALL, NOT ANY (none may match) or NOT ALL.
  • Position: where the text goes: Before Character Defs, After Character Defs, Top of Author's Note, Bottom of Author's Note, @ Depth (inside the chat, a number of messages back, with a Role), Top of Example Msgs, Bottom of Example Msgs, or Outlet (named macro) (wherever a prompt writes {{outlet::name}}).
  • Insertion Order: lower numbers go higher up.
  • Trigger %: the chance the entry is sent when it matches (with Use trigger %).

Advanced

  • Recursion: whether this entry's text can trigger other entries, can be triggered by them, or waits for recursion (Non-recursable (won't be activated by others), Prevent further recursion, Delay until recursion).
  • Inclusion Group: entries sharing a group label compete, and only one is sent: by Group weight, by group scoring, or always the one marked Prioritize (group override).
  • Scan Overrides (blank = use global): this entry's own scan depth, case sensitivity and whole-word matching.
  • Timed Effects (blank = off): Sticky (stays in for so many messages after it fires), Cooldown (sits out for so many after), Delay (cannot fire until the chat has so many messages).
  • Generation Types (none = every kind): fire only for some kinds of request, such as normal replies, continues or regenerations.
  • Character Filter (blank = everyone): only for certain characters or tags, or never for them.
  • Additional Matching Sources: also look for keywords in your persona's description or in the card's description, personality, Character's Note, scenario or creator's notes.
  • Ignore token budget sends the entry even when the lore budget is used up.

Scan settings

How entries are matched applies to every chat. Open it from the gear in a chat's Lorebooks window (Scan settings (they govern every chat)):

SettingDefaultWhat it does
Scan depthLast 4 messagesHow many recent messages are searched for keywords
Whole words onlyonA keyword must match whole words ("bat" does not fire on "bathroom")
Case-sensitiveoffCapitals must match
Scan speaker namesonSpeaker names count, so a character's name can fire their entry
Match like SillyTavernoffMatches keywords exactly as SillyTavern does
Show placeholder booksoffLists names bound to no book yet
Recursive scanonEntries that were sent can fire other entries
Max recursion steps0Limits how deep that goes; 0 is no limit
Budget (% of context)25The share of the context lore may use; 0 switches the limit off
Budget cap (tokens)0A ceiling in tokens on top; 0 is none
Show why entries firedonShows the reasons in the Lorebooks window

The chat's Lorebooks window

Lorebooks in a conversation's header shows every book that reaches the chat and every entry in them, marked as in the prompt right now, always sent, not matched, or switched off. With Show why entries fired on, chips say why each fired (the message number that matched, the entry that triggered it, its chance roll) or why a matching entry was held back (over the lore budget, lost its roll or its group, cooling down, the wrong kind of request).

  1. Filter entries.
  2. In Prompt: only the entries in the prompt right now.
  3. Opens every entry the filter shows, or closes them again.
  4. Add: links or binds a book: to the Character (every chat with it), to This Chat, or Everywhere.
  5. The scan settings, which govern every chat. See Scan settings.
  6. A book's row: its chips show how it reaches the chat (clicking one removes that link), the count says how many of its entries are in the prompt, and adds an entry.
  7. An entry's state: in the prompt right now, always sent, not matched, or switched off.
  8. Edits the entry in place, and its switch turns it on or off.

Click an entry to read it. Changes here are saved at once.

When the card carries its own lorebook that is not in your library yet, the button gets an orange dot (in a narrow window, the More button it waits behind wears it, and so does a group you filed it in; on a phone, the options button and the Lorebooks tile in its menu wear it) and the window offers Import or Leave It Embedded.

Lorebooks on a character

Linked lorebooks

A character can have several linked books, in order. The Details tab shows them; on the Edit tab (unlocked), Linked Lorebooks lists them, Add links another, and the × unlinks one (the book itself is kept). The first linked book is the one named inside the character's exported card.

The embedded lorebook

The Embedded Lorebook section of the Edit tab edits the book inside the card: each entry's keys, secondary keys, content, Selective, Constant, order and priority, and its switch, which turns the entry on or off and says On or Off beside it. Edit lorebook in expanded view opens it larger, where Collapse All folds each entry to its name and keys. Edits land with Save Changes.

  • Insert from Library copies a library book's entries into the card (replacing any book it had).
  • Send to Library puts the saved embedded book in your library and links it to the character, so chats use it. If a book with that name exists, you can link that one instead.
  • Remove Book empties it.

Importing a card never creates a library book on its own; the card's book stays embedded until you send it to the library (or import it from the chat's Lorebooks window).

Lorebooks in one chat

To bind books to one chat, use Lorebooks in a chat's right-click menu in the Chats view, the book button on a chat in a character's Chats tab, or Add > This Chat in the conversation's Lorebooks window. Click books to bind or unbind them; Unbind All clears the chat's list.

Generating entries with AI

Generate with AI (beside New, for a new book) or the AI button in an open book (Generate entries with AI, to add to it) opens a three-step generator:

  1. Source material: Paste Text, Web / Wiki (a page address and Fetch; wiki category pages list their pages to choose from, and Detail per page sets how much of each page is read), or From Library (characters' fields and chats).
  2. Instructions, optional: what you want from the entries.
  3. Output: the Connection, how many entries at most, and how much text each request may carry (Context).

Large sources are split across several requests. The results open for review (Review generated entries): edit, regenerate or discard each, then Add Selected. Nothing is saved until you press Save on the book.

  1. Select all, or clear them all.
  2. Expand all, or close them all.
  3. An entry's switch: whether it is added.
  4. Regenerate this entry.
  5. Discard this entry.

Lorebooks and card updates

When a linked character's card is checked for updates, its embedded lorebook is compared entry by entry with the site's. Applying an update changes only the card's embedded book; the books in your library are never touched by an update check. See Checking for updates.

Browsing lorebooks online

ChubAI, Wyvern and Botbooru publish lorebooks too. In the Online view, the toggle beside the site picker switches between characters and lorebooks (Browse this site's lorebooks), and Import puts a book in your library. See Browsing online.

Personas

A persona is your side of a chat: the name the character calls you and a description of who you are. Its name fills {{user}} and its description {{persona}} in prompts and in card previews, and the prompt's Persona description block sends the description to the model.

You can keep several personas. One is your global persona, used everywhere; any chat can be set to use a different one.

Managing personas

Settings > Chat > Personas lists them. The circle on a row (Make active) makes that persona your global one; the pen edits it; New Persona adds one. The first persona you create becomes the global one by itself. Below it, In the conversation points to the chat header's windows, where everything else about a chat is set.

A persona has:

  • a name and a description (how the character should see you);
  • pronouns (below);
  • a picture and bound lorebooks, which are set from a chat's Personas window (below).

With no persona at all, {{user}} is left as written in previews; in prompts it falls back to the name stored with the chat, or "User".

In a chat

Personas in a conversation's header shows who you are in this chat and why: your global persona, or one set for this chat only.

  1. Who you are in this chat, and why.
  2. A persona: click it to edit it, below.
  3. Be this person in this chat only: uses it in this chat alone.
  4. Be this person everywhere: makes it your global persona.
  5. Delete this persona.
  6. New Persona.

While the chat has a persona of its own, Use Global at the top puts it back on your global persona.

Clicking a persona edits it here, with more than the Settings editor offers:

  • Add a picture (or Replace picture / Remove): any image up to 25 MB, shown beside your messages.
  • Lorebooks: books bound to the persona (see below).

The window keeps up with changes made elsewhere: a persona added, renamed, given a new picture or deleted in another tab, on another device or in Settings shows here without reopening it, and over your messages in the chat too.

Closing the window, or picking another persona, with changes you have not saved asks first: Keep Editing keeps them, Discard lets them go.

If the persona you are editing is changed somewhere else meanwhile (the Assistant applying a persona edit, another tab, another device), the editor shows it as it is now when you have not typed in it yet. Once you have, your typing stays and Changed elsewhere says so: press Reload to see the saved version before saving, since saving over it is refused until you do.

A chat whose persona differs from your global one shows it on its card in the Chats view. Deleting a persona that a chat uses puts that chat back on your global persona.

Lorebooks

A persona can carry lorebooks: pick them under Lorebooks in the persona's editor in a chat's Personas window, or bind them from the Lorebooks manager. They are active in any chat where that persona is in force, alongside the character's own books and the global ones. See Lorebooks.

Pronouns

The Pronouns fold in a persona's editor sets how cards and prompts refer to you. Pick She, He, They, It, or Custom and fill in the five forms: subject, object, possessive, possessive pronoun and reflexive.

Cards and prompts use them through {{sub}}, {{obj}}, {{poss}}, {{poss_p}} and {{ref}}, which follow the capitals you write them with: {{Sub}} starts a sentence ("She"). A form left empty, or no persona at all, gives the they/them word. The full table is in Macros.

Bringing personas across

The SillyTavern migration brings personas with their names, descriptions, pictures and one bound lorebook each, and makes SillyTavern's default persona your global one. See Bringing a SillyTavern library.

Macros

Macros are placeholders in double braces that are filled in when a prompt is built. They work in card fields, prompt manager blocks, your persona's description, lorebook entries (their keys and content), the Author's note, a preset's Preset Settings, and the messages you send (filled in once, when you send). The card's first message is filled in when a chat starts.

Macro names ignore capitals, so {{Char}} and {{char}} are the same macro, with one exception: the pronoun macros copy the capitals you write (below). A macro that nothing fills is sent as written; the prompt inspector flags those.

Outside a chat (card previews in the library, for example) only {{user}}, {{char}} and the pronoun macros are filled in.

Names and the card

MacroBecomes
{{user}}Your persona's name
{{char}}The character's name
{{persona}}Your persona's description
{{description}} or {{charDescription}}The card's description
{{personality}} or {{charPersonality}}The card's personality
{{scenario}} or {{charScenario}}The card's scenario (or this chat's own, when it has one)
{{charPrompt}}The card's system prompt
{{charInstruction}}The card's post-history instructions
{{charDepthPrompt}}The card's Character's Note
{{creatorNotes}} or {{charCreatorNotes}}The card's creator's notes
{{greeting}} or {{charFirstMessage}}The first message; {{greeting::2}} is the second alternate greeting
{{version}} or {{charVersion}}The card's version
{{mesExamples}}The example dialogue, split into its examples
{{mesExamplesRaw}}The example dialogue as written
{{original}}Inside a card's system prompt or post-history instructions, the preset's own text they replace
{{authorsNote}}This chat's Author's note
{{defaultAuthorsNote}}The Author's note new chats start with
{{notChar}}Your name (in a one-character chat, everyone who is not the character)
{{group}}, {{charIfNotGroup}}, {{groupNotMuted}}The character's name (chats have one character)

Pronouns

Your persona's pronouns, set in its editor (see Personas):

MacroFormWhen the persona has none
{{sub}}Subjectthey
{{obj}}Objectthem
{{poss}}Possessivetheir
{{poss_p}}Possessive pronountheirs
{{ref}}Reflexivethemselves

Write the macro the way the word should come out: {{sub}} gives the form as you stored it, {{Sub}} capitalizes it ("She"), and {{SUB}} gives capitals ("SHE").

Chat and generation

MacroBecomes
{{lastMessage}}, {{lastMessageId}}The last message, and its number
{{lastUserMessage}}, {{lastCharMessage}}Your last message, the character's last message (hidden ones skipped)
{{firstIncludedMessageId}}The number of the oldest message that fits the context budget
{{firstDisplayedMessageId}}The number of the first message shown
{{lastSwipeId}}, {{currentSwipeId}}How many alternates the last message has, and which one is showing (from 1)
{{allChatRange}}The range of message numbers, such as 0-41
{{model}}The model answering
{{maxContext}}, {{maxResponse}}, {{maxPrompt}}The context budget, the reply's reserve, and what is left for the prompt
{{lastGenerationType}}What was asked for: normal, regenerate, swipe, continue or impersonate
{{input}}The text you typed as direction for a directed reply or "write my next message"
{{isMobile}}true when you are on a phone
{{hasExtension::id}}true when the extension with that id is on

Time

Times are on your device's clock.

MacroBecomes
{{time}}The time; {{time::UTC+2}} in another zone
{{date}}, {{weekday}}The date and the day of the week
{{isotime}}, {{isodate}}The time and date as 14:05 and 2024-01-31
{{datetimeformat::format}}The date and time in your own format (for example {{datetimeformat::dddd, MMMM D}})
{{timeDiff::a::b}}The time between two dates, in words
{{idleDuration}}How long ago your previous message was sent

Text helpers

MacroBecomes
{{// note}} or {{comment::note}}Nothing: a note to yourself that is never sent
{{newline}}, {{newline::3}}One or more line breaks
{{space}}, {{space::3}}One or more spaces
{{trim}}Removes the line breaks around it; {{trim}}text{{/trim}} trims the text inside
{{noop}}Nothing
{{reverse::text}}The text backwards
{{outlet::name}}The lorebook entries placed in the outlet called "name" (see Lorebooks)

Write \{ and \} for literal braces.

Chance

MacroBecomes
{{roll::2d6+1}} or {{roll::20}}A dice roll
{{random::a::b::c}} or {{random::a,b,c}}One of the choices, picked afresh every time the prompt is built
{{pick::a::b::c}}One of the choices, the same one every time in this chat and place

Conditions

{{if::condition::text}} gives the text when the condition holds. The longer form spans lines and has an else:

{{if persona}}
Some text about you.
{{else}}
Nothing is known about you.
{{/if}}

A condition is false when it is empty or reads off, false or 0. Put ! before it to turn it around. A bare macro name as the condition ({{if persona}}) is filled in first.

Variables

Variables remember a value between replies. Local ones belong to the chat; global ones are shared by every chat.

LocalGlobalDoes
{{setvar::name::value}}{{setglobalvar::name::value}}Sets it
{{getvar::name}}{{getglobalvar::name}}Gives its value
{{addvar::name::value}}{{addglobalvar::name::value}}Adds a number, or appends text
{{incvar::name}}, {{decvar::name}}{{incglobalvar::name}}, {{decglobalvar::name}}Adds or takes away one
{{hasvar::name}}{{hasglobalvar::name}}true when it is set
{{deletevar::name}}{{deleteglobalvar::name}}Removes it

A shorthand covers the same ground: {{.name}} reads a local variable and {{$name}} a global one, and operators follow the name, for example {{.count++}}, {{.mood = happy}} or {{.hp -= 3}}. Only a real reply saves changes to variables; the prompt inspector works on a copy.

Extension macros

Extensions can add macros. Among the bundled ones, Memory adds {{memory}} (and {{summary}}), and Trackers adds {{trackers}}, for placing their text exactly where a preset wants it.

Browsing online

The Online view browses the character sites from inside the app: search, sort, filter, preview a card in full, and import it with its online link kept, so you can check it for updates later. Every request to a site goes from your browser or your server straight to that site; Project Σ runs no service in between.

These sites are run by other people and change without notice. When a site changes its API, its browsing can break until the app catches up.

The sites

SiteOn at firstWhat an account adds
ChubAIyesOptional token: following, your favorites, restricted content
JannyAIyesNone needed; full card text needs the Hosted Browser
CharacterTavernyesOptional session: NSFW content
PygmalionyesOptional login: NSFW content and following
WyvernyesOptional login: following and the recommended sort
BotbooruyesOptional login: NSFW, favorites, follows, favorite tags
JanitorAI (beta)noNeeded past the first page, for following, and for hidden definitions; also needs the Hosted Browser
DataCat (beta, deprecated)noNone needed
RisuAI Realm (beta)no, it comes with an extensionNone needed

Choosing your sites

Settings > Online > Provider Order & Defaults lists the sites. Drag them into the order you like; the Online tab opens on the first one unless View Defaults names another. The eye on each row shows or hides that site in the Online tab, and each row can set the view and sort the site opens with and whether it starts with Hide Owned or Hide Possible. Turning on a beta site shows a short note about what it needs first.

The RisuAI Realm arrives with its bundled extension: switch on RisuAI Realm under Settings > Extensions and it appears here.

With two or more sites on, a menu at the top of the Online view switches between them. On a phone, Switch Provider in the More sheet does the same.

Browsing

Each site's bar has its own search box, sorts and filters, matched to what that site offers:

  1. The site you are browsing. See Choosing your sites.
  2. Browse this site's lorebooks, on the sites that have them. See Lorebooks online.
  3. Browse everything, or Following: new characters from the creators you follow.
  4. The sort. Where a list has no sort to choose, the sort stays in its place, greyed, and says how the list is ordered: a following feed that comes in one order shows it (Latest Updates on ChubAI's timeline, Newest Created on Wyvern, Feed Order on JanitorAI), and a creator's characters show Creator's Sort (Uploader's Sort on Botbooru), since their sorts sit in the creator's bar, by their name.
  5. Tags.
  6. Features.
  7. SFW Only: the NSFW switch.
  8. Refresh.
  9. The search box, and its arrow to search.
  10. The search for creators.
  11. A character: click it for its preview, below. marks one already in your library.

The same controls on every site, matched to what that site offers:

  • Search: by name or keyword. Several sites also accept a link to a character page, which opens that character directly, and most have a separate search for creators.
  • Sort: the site's own orders, such as trending, newest or most downloaded. Each order wears an icon, the same one on every site (a flame for hot, a calendar for newest), so a menu reads at a glance.
  • Tags: include or exclude tags (most sites cycle each tag through included, excluded and neutral), with extra options such as token limits where the site supports them.
  • Features: on/off filters such as "must have a lorebook" or "exclude forks", and on every site Hide Owned Characters and Hide Possible Matches.
  • The NSFW switch, remembered per site.

Switching between Browse and Following, or into a creator's characters, never rearranges the bar: every control keeps its place. One that cannot apply to the list you are on stays greyed, and hovering it (or tapping it on a phone) says why. Tags keep working in Following, filtering the characters it brings in. While filters are on, Tags and Features show how many with a small number on their corner, so the bar never shifts as you pick them.

More results load as you scroll. Settings > Online > Infinite Scroll turns that off per site, for a Load More button instead.

Exclude Tags in each site's settings section hides tags you never want to see from every search on that site.

Creators and following

Click a creator's name to browse only their characters, with the creator's own sorts and a Follow button. Sites that support following (ChubAI, JanitorAI, Pygmalion, Wyvern, Botbooru and DataCat) have a following view listing new characters from the creators you follow; Manage there lists them, adds one by name or link, and unfollows.

In Library and Possible Match

Characters you already own show an In Library badge, matched by the online link your character carries.

Characters that look like one you own get a Possible Match badge, on any site: the same card re-uploaded elsewhere is the common case. The badge weighs who made the card, the site's own description of it when the site shows one, and how common the name is in your library: owning twenty different characters called "Narrator" does not flag every Narrator online, while the same creator, the same description or a long, unusual name does. A character of the same name that you own from another page on the same site is weighed by its public description: the same description counts fully, a different one not at all (creators often publish several cards of one name), and when there is nothing to compare the badge shows dimmed as a possible match. The brighter the badge, the surer the match. Settings > Online > Possible Match Detection > Sensitivity (0 to 100, default 65) sets how readily it appears; lower flags more. The badge is only a hint: importing still runs a full comparison against your library (see The duplicate warning).

Hide Owned Characters and Hide Possible Matches in a site's Features menu remove those cards from the grid.

Previews and importing

Click a card for its preview: the full text the site provides (description, scenario, greetings, examples, creator's notes), its stats, its lorebook and gallery when it has them, and Import. Settings > General > Character Details > Collapse all browse preview sections by default starts each preview folded.

  1. The picture: click it to see it full size.
  2. The creator's name: their other characters. See Creators and following.
  3. The creator's profile on the site.
  4. Adds the character to your favorites on the site, with your account there.
  5. Open: the character's page on the site.
  6. Import.
  7. A section's heading: folds its text or opens it.

Every site's preview has this layout. Only ChubAI and Botbooru have the heart, and Botbooru names the card's writer and its uploader apart.

Import first checks your library for the character. With Import in the background on (Settings > Media, on by default), the preview closes at once and the import joins a queue, one at a time per site, followed in the notifications bell, where a failure can be retried. Off, the preview waits for the import.

An import that could not fetch part of a character still comes in, and a warning names what it came in without: its picture (it gets a plain placeholder), a linked lorebook on ChubAI, or on CharacterTavern the card file (its picture, and any alternate greetings, tags or lorebook only that file carries) or, when the file came but would not read, the card inside it. On JannyAI and Pygmalion, when the character's page could not be read, only what the search listing shows comes in, and the warning says it came in without its definition: delete it and import it again later.

A card with media beyond its picture (images its text links to, a gallery on the site, galleries on image hosts) is handled by When an import has extra media, also under Settings > Media:

  • Download in the Background (the default): queued for the background downloader while you carry on.
  • Ask Me (Show a Download Prompt): a window lists what there is, with Download All.
  • Do Nothing.

Most people want an imported character to arrive complete, so the media downloads by itself. To be asked first, or to skip it, change When an import has extra media under Settings > Media.

Lorebooks online

ChubAI, Wyvern and Botbooru also publish lorebooks. The toggle beside the site menu (Browse this site's lorebooks) switches the Online view to them: search and sort, preview a book, and Import it into your library. See Lorebooks.

Accounts and logins

Logins are set in each site's section under Settings > Online, and some sites also ask in place when you reach for a feature that needs one. Tokens and passwords are kept with your account's settings on the server.

SiteWhereWhat you enter
ChubAIAuthenticationURQL Token: the URQL_TOKEN value from chub.ai's local storage while you are signed in there; the check button beside it confirms ChubAI knows the token and names your account
JanitorAIStep 2: Account LoginEmail and Password (Sign In runs through the Hosted Browser), or a session token in Token with Use Token
CharacterTavernSession CookiesCookie String from a signed-in browser; Auto keep-alive refreshes it
PygmalionLoginEmail and Password; Remember credentials (auto-refresh token) keeps you signed in
WyvernLoginEmail and Password
BotbooruLoginUsername and Password, or a token pasted in the login window

For JanitorAI, use a throwaway account: recovering hidden definitions acts on the account (see below).

The Hosted Browser

Some sites (JanitorAI, and JannyAI's card pages) sit behind Cloudflare, which only lets real browsers through. The Hosted Browser is that browser, shared by every site that needs it. Set it up under Settings > Online > Hosted Browser, where Browser picks one of two:

  • Run One for Me (Recommended): the server starts a hidden Chrome, Chromium or Edge on its own machine the first time something needs it, and stops it after 10 minutes idle. Start Now and Stop control it by hand. With accounts on, only admins can use it; others use their own browser instead.
  • Use My Own Browser Endpoint: point Endpoint at a Chrome DevTools address, such as a Chrome started with --remote-debugging-port=9222. This suits a server without a screen or graphics, or an account on a shared server. Project Σ's download comes with a ready-made endpoint to run on another machine, the hosted-browser tool, with its own instructions.

The test button (Test this browser) checks the browser: that it answers, that it can run pages, its codecs and graphics. Graphics rows are only hints. The decider is each site's own check, Check janitorai.com and Check jannyai.com in their sections, which tries to get past Cloudflare for real: a pass there means the site works. Whether a given machine passes varies, so check rather than guess.

Signing in and getting past Cloudflare are separate: a login does not get you past Cloudflare, and getting past Cloudflare does not sign you in.

Extensions can use the Hosted Browser too. Under Extension Sites in the same section, every site an extension you switched on has registered is listed with its own Check. That list is what each extension declared, not a limit: an extension has full access, so it can use the browser anywhere. Such an extension drives the browser with every login it holds on that site, so the warnings in Using extensions apply.

Notes per site

ChubAI

Browsing and importing need no account. A token adds the following view, My Favorites and the favorite button. The following view is ChubAI's own timeline: the latest updates from the creators and tags you follow, newest first, loading as you scroll. Build the timeline from every followed creator (in Settings > Online > ChubAI) lists each followed creator's newest characters instead, in an order you choose from the sort menu; it asks ChubAI once per creator, so it loads slower the more creators you follow. ChubAI is the one site with version history: a linked character's version history lists every version published there. ChubAI's lorebooks can be browsed too.

JanitorAI

First-party access to janitorai.com, off by default (beta). It needs the Hosted Browser, and an account for anything past the first page of results, for following, and for hidden definitions. Settings walks through both: Step 1: Browser and Step 2: Account Login. A sign-in that leaves no session says why: JanitorAI's own message, Cloudflare's human check not passed on the sign-in form (most often a VPN), or a sign-in that never left the form.

Many JanitorAI characters hide their definition. Their preview says so and offers Extract Now, which recovers the definition by having the site build a prompt for a chat with the character and reading it back. For that one request, on your JanitorAI account, it creates a throwaway persona with a random name, a temporary API preset pointed at an address that goes nowhere (so nothing is sent to any model), and one chat with one message; all of it is removed and your settings restored afterwards. On an account that has never chatted, your profile name is set to the same random name for the request and cleared again. If JanitorAI refuses to put any of it back, a warning says what to fix on janitorai.com. When the creator has also turned off outside API use, Try Anyway asks JanitorAI's own model to repeat the definition back instead; check what comes back, since it is the model's copy rather than the creator's text. Importing a character with a hidden definition runs the extraction first.

In the Update Checks group, Recover hidden definitions on update checks (off by default) does the same during update checks. In DataCat Migration, Migrate DataCat Links... turns characters linked through DataCat into JanitorAI links where it can.

JannyAI

A mirror of JanitorAI with no account and no following. Browsing and search work without setup, but a card's full definition lives behind Cloudflare: without the Hosted Browser, an import gets only the name, picture, tags and blurb. Press Check jannyai.com after setting up the browser. JannyAI's Cloudflare can be stricter than JanitorAI's; if the managed browser fails, point the Hosted Browser at a browser of your own.

CharacterTavern

NSFW content needs a session: paste the cookie string from a browser signed in to CharacterTavern. Filters include having a lorebook and being an original character. The sort menu offers CharacterTavern's own orders: Popular, Best, New & Noteworthy, Top Rated, Hidden Gems, Newest and Recently Updated. Search results show a card's tokens; its likes, tags and alternate greetings appear in the preview once the character's page loads.

Pygmalion

NSFW content and following need a login. Pygmalion follows creators by user id.

Wyvern

Browsing, searching and the Popular NSFW sort work without an account; a login adds following and the Recommended sort. Wyvern's lorebooks can be browsed too.

Botbooru

A tag-driven catalogue. Without an account you browse SFW cards; logging in adds NSFW (and NSFL, with Include NSFL posts), your favorites, following and your account's tag blacklist. Favorite Tags boosts tags in the Curated sort; Weighted tag mode (experimental) gives each tag a weight from -1000 to 1000 instead, with always-follow and always-block, and the sort menu then lists Curated (Recent), Curated (Tag Score) and Curated (Only Followed). Under Downloads, Report downloads to Botbooru (on by default) counts your imports on the site.

DataCat

Deprecated and off by default. DataCat mirrors JanitorAI and can extract characters it does not have yet (Extract). Some of its sorts come from JanitorAI behind Cloudflare and are often unreachable; its own sorts work without setup.

Its section in Settings:

  • Session: the anonymous Token the app uses to browse, made for you. New Token makes another and Clear Session drops it.
  • Extraction: Publish extracted characters on DataCat puts what you extract on DataCat's public feed (off keeps it private), and Re-extract characters on update checks asks DataCat for fresh data before each comparison, which is slower.
  • JanitorAI Login (Hampter pagination): JanitorAI shows only the first page of the sorts it serves to visitors without an account. Paste a JanitorAI session Cookie and press Save Login to page further.
  • Card Maintenance: Restore Original Avatars finds DataCat-linked characters that still carry DataCat's smaller picture, shows the current and original side by side, and replaces the ones you pick.

RisuAI Realm

Comes with the bundled RisuAI Realm extension, off until you switch it on. Search accepts tag:name terms. The full definition, greetings and lorebook arrive with the import; update checks are not available.

Linking characters to their source

A character linked to its online page can be checked for updates, can download its gallery from the site, and shows In Library when you browse. Imports from the Online view and from links are linked automatically.

To link one by hand, click Link on its Details tab (or Link to Provider in its card menu).

  1. The name and creator to search for.
  2. All Providers, or the one site to search.
  3. Searches; pick the right result to link it.
  4. A character page's link, pasted.
  5. Link this URL.

For a linked character, the same place opens its link window: View on the site, Download Gallery (for sites with galleries), Version History, Update Lock (Skip this character during update checks) and Unlink (which saves a snapshot first).

Auto-Link Characters

More Options > Auto-Link Characters searches your sites for every unlinked character in your library and sorts the results into Confident, Needs Review and No Match, by the same rule as Find Duplicates. Confident matches have the same name (a version tag such as "(v2)" aside) and the same creator, or, when either side has no creator, the character's own text; they are ticked for you. For each one needing review, pick the right match. Nothing is linked until you press Link Selected. Stop pauses the scan, and you can resume it while the app stays open.

  1. Confident, Needs Review and No Match, each with its count.
  2. Select All and Deselect All.
  3. A character's box: tick it to link it.
  4. Opens the character's possible matches, to pick the right one.
  5. Link Selected.

Checking for updates

For a linked character, Check for Updates in its card menu compares your copy with the current card on the site, field by field. Open a long field to see your version and the site's side by side. Tick the changes you want and press Apply, which counts them. A change that would empty a field you filled is marked empties your field. With auto-snapshots on, the character's state before the update is saved to its version history.

  1. The character's name: opens it.
  2. Ticks every change, or none.
  3. A change's box: whether it is applied.
  4. Opens a long change to compare your version and the site's.
  5. Apply, counting the ticked changes.

To check many at once, select them and press Updates, or use Settings > Online > Card Updates > Check All for Updates for every linked character. First pick which fields to compare (comparing tags often turns up differences you do not care about), then Start Check. Each character's row says what was found and names the changed fields, and View opens its changes. Tabs above the list show only the characters with changes, up to date, failed, unavailable, locked or updated, once there are any.

  1. Ticks every character shown, or none.
  2. A character's box: whether it is updated with the others.
  3. The character's name: opens it.
  4. View: its changes.
  5. Update Lock: locked, the character is skipped by update checks.

Cancel stops the check before the next character (one it was still fetching waits with the rest), and the same button then offers Resume for them; with none left, it is Close. Review opens a summary of every change the ticked characters would take, with any change that would empty a field listed separately, and Apply to in the summary applies them (both buttons count the characters).

A character with Update Lock on is skipped. Embedded lorebooks are compared entry by entry; applying replaces the card's embedded book only (see Lorebooks and card updates).

When a site fails to load

The grid shows what went wrong, with Retry and Copy Error Report. The report is ready to paste into a bug report: it includes the app version, the site, the error and your settings as on/off flags only, never your tokens.

Settings and themes

Using Settings

More Options > Settings opens Settings, with its sections down the side: General, Online, Media, AI, Chat, Extensions, Account, Developer and Info. Search... at the top finds a setting in any section. On a phone, Settings opens on a list of its sections, each with a line on what it holds, under Search...; tap one to open it, and back returns to the list.

Most settings wait for Save Settings at the bottom. A few apply the moment you change them: UI Scale, Modal Size, Button Style, Mobile Layout, and every button that runs something (Back Up Now, Reset window layouts, and the like). The theme, custom CSS, connections, personas and extensions have their own save buttons. Closing Settings with unsaved changes asks whether to discard them.

Restore Defaults puts every setting back as it shipped, after listing what you would lose (your theme, custom CSS, tag rules, saved prompts and window layouts, among others). Saved logins and tokens are kept, and so are your extensions' switches.

Settings belong to your account and follow you to every device, except Mobile Layout, which is kept per device.

Where each section is covered:

SectionCovered in
Generalbelow, and the start page in Getting started, search defaults in Search and filters, duplicates in The library, display names in Character details, version history in Character details, backups in Data and backups
OnlineBrowsing online
MediaMedia and galleries
AIConnections
ChatPersonas (the rest of a chat's settings live in its own windows, see Chat windows)
ExtensionsExtensions
AccountAccounts and server settings, and the migration in Bringing a SillyTavern library

The theme

Settings > General > Theme & Layout > Theme opens the theme editor, with a live preview of a dialog, a window, a conversation and buttons. Nothing changes for good until Save.

Start from offers presets: Glass, Frosted, Solid, High contrast and Classic. Then adjust the groups of settings:

  • Accent: the accent colour.
  • Surfaces: glass opacity, blur, borders, shadows, the app and panel backgrounds, the top bar, glow, grain and vignette.
  • Phone surfaces: opacity and blur on a phone.
  • Header: the tint, colour and fade of window headers.
  • Text: the body, secondary, faint and muted text colours.
  • Shape & type: corner radius, density, text size, the interface font (Noto Sans (default), System, Segoe UI, Humanist, Serif or Monospace) and line spacing.
  • Conversation header: its fill and blur.
  • Transcript: message text, speech, narration and emphasis colours, the character and user message plates, avatar size and the transcript font.

Each setting has a reset button. Every token at the bottom opens every colour and size the app's stylesheet defines, for full control.

Export saves your theme as a file and Import loads one (an imported theme applies when you save). Reset Defaults puts the draft back to the shipped theme. Closing with unsaved changes asks what to do.

Themes can also come from extensions: the bundled Midnight theme brings palettes, a sky and a layout of its own, and the bundled Σ theme finishes the app's own design with calm palettes and the chat windows' material on every surface. Each is switched on under Settings > Extensions, and your own theme and custom CSS still apply over it.

Scale, size and layout

Under Settings > General > Theme & Layout:
